Every named individual this archive has found

The Register

Every person of this surname, and every spouse and parent of one, met in the course of the research — searchable, grouped by the spelling the record used, with the source that names each one. This is the raw catch, not a family tree, and most of it is not this family.

The People the people themselves, with what is known of each Every Name every name, and how many bore it — a name is not a person The Registeryou are here one row per source line: the raw catch, and mostly not this family

356 entries for 356 distinct names, 35 of them with a link to the source that names them; the other 321 say in the Source column why no link exists.

Read this before you read the list

The surname is rare. Forebears counts about 2,124 Lerenas worldwide, with the highest density anywhere in Uruguay — the signature of a small founding group that emigrated. At that scale most people carrying the name probably are related to one another at some depth. But “probably related” is not evidence, and a shared surname is not a relationship.

Most entries here come from surname sweeps — every Lerena in the CEMLA arrival index, in the Argentine national censuses of 1869 and 1895, in the Uruguayan and Argentine parish and civil registers, in the South African death, estate and cemetery indexes. They are here because they were found, not because they are kin. None is claimed as kin.

The list is grouped by the spelling the record used — Lerena, Llerena, Larena, Lorena, Lerina — because the spelling is evidence in itself: it is how a clerk heard the name, and the clerks disagreed. Names in other groups are spouses and parents who married in or are named alongside.

Three marks are used. Connected — proven to the direct line. Excluded — checked and shown not to belong. Unplaced — found, recorded, and honestly unresolved. Most are unplaced, and that is the point of the page.

The moment more of Pablo Armando’s people are named, this becomes the place to look — several of these may snap into position at once, and the work of finding them will already have been done. The card that named his parents →

Rosalia AGUILAR (mother of Sancho)
Connected		Spain, presumably Andalusia · Spanish · found in Spain
NAMED ON AN ACT, 15 September 2026 - the first time this archive has had a generation above Sancho on anything but a tree. The baptism of their grandson LUIS ESCOLASTICO at the Iglesia Matriz of Montevideo, 10 February 1811, gives "Abuelos paternos FELIZ GERENA y ROSALIA AGUILAR" in the same sentence that calls their son "Don SANCHO LLERENA natural de ANDALUCIA" . GERENA is the machine's G-for-L - a seventh spelling for this family, and the same mangling that produced the still-unresolved "Dona Andugara GERENA" in the Santa Rosa de Lima confirmations at Rosario. SHE IS THE REASON THIS FAMILY IS CALLED 'LERENA Y AGUILAR' AT ALL. For weeks the matronymic was marked unattested - none of the fourteen Uruguayan records gave Sancho one - and on 14 September this archive built a whole Spanish hypothesis on the idea that AGUILAR came from a GRANDMOTHER, the Ana Maria de Aguilar who married at Santa Ana, Granada in 1754. It comes from his MOTHER, which is simply how Spanish naming works, and the act says so. WHAT IS STILL NOT KNOWN ABOUT THEM: everything else. No dates, no places, no marriage, no other children. The Geneanet pages carry nothing but the line "Family: Revista 15 IEGU", and one Spanish tree offers Cordoba about 1725 and a marriage about 1753 - all values marked "about", with no source at all, and giving Sancho a birth of ca 1754 that the 1812 padron contradicts. So the PARENTAGE is documented and the PEOPLE are not. The search that follows from this is FELIX LERENA and ROSALIA AGUILAR in CORDOBA and it has not been run against Spanish parish records. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT since 14 September. Film 007713375 image 02410, Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, the baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 February 1811 - the only record in this archive that names Sancho's own parents. The plate reads 'y Ros[alia] ... lia Aguilar' across the line break - her name survives the check exactly as the transcript gave it.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Montevideo, Iglesia Matriz, baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 Feb 1811 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ, ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X7F6-M" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X7F6-M</a>; plus Geneanet trees citing Revista 15 IEGU - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713375 image 02410

ANTONIO (enslaved in Sancho Lerena's household, 1812)
Unplaced		found in Montevideo
NAMED ON THE PADRON ITSELF, and this archive said the padron named nobody. Entered as 'Antonio, negro esclavo' - the only one of the four whose status the enumerator wrote out - immediately after Sancho's youngest child Dominga. Nothing else about him is known: no age, no baptism, no death, no emancipation. He is here because he was a person and the record has his name.		record ↗

Aurelio Eusebio BARROS Lerena
Unplaced		1833-09-14 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A SECOND CHILD OF JUSTA PASTORA LERENA AND MANUEL ZEFERINO BARROS, found 21 September 2026 - and he existed on a plate this archive had cited for a week without opening. The register carried the GODFATHER of this act since 14 September and never the CHILD. The act reads: 'En diez y nueve de Octubre de mil ochocientos treinta y tres, yo el D.r D.n JUAN CIRIACO OTAEGUI, con licencia del Cura y Vicario D.r D. DAMASO LARRANAGA, bautice solemnemente a AURELIO EUSEBIO, que nacio el dia CATORCE del proximo pasado, hijo de Man.l ZEFERINO BARROS, natural de PERNAMBUCO, y de JUSTA PASTORA LERENA, natural de MONTEVIDEO ... Padrinos ABELINO LERENA y DOMINGA LERENA.' So he is a brother of JUAN FACUNDO FRUTUOSO, born 27 November 1831, and a grandson of Sancho, and his godparents are his mother's own brother and sister. AND HIS MOTHER'S BIRTHPLACE IS GIVEN AS MONTEVIDEO HERE AND AS CANELONES ON HIS BROTHER'S ACT OF 1831 - two acts, two years apart, one parish, disagreeing about where she was born. Neither is preferred here; the Canelones reading has the support of her sister Dominga's baptism there in 1804.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Catedral de Montevideo, baptism of 19 October 1833 - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713376 images 01176-01177; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-YSKW-T" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-YSKW-T</a>
Hilario BARROS Llerena
Unplaced		1835 · Canelones · Uruguayan · found in Canelones
A THIRD CHILD OF JUSTA PASTORA AND MANUEL ZEFERINO BARROS, found 21 September 2026 - and found only because the index date was impossible. FamilySearch indexes his christening as 19 April 1726, which is the first year of the collection and not a date; the same artefact put his cousin Carlos Ambrosio Florencio in 1726 earlier the same day. THE PLATE GIVES THE REAL YEAR: 'El dia DIEZ Y NUEVE DE ABRIL del ano de mil ochocientos t[reinta y cinco], yo Don FRANCISCO LARROBLA, Cura y Vicario de esta Parroquia de los Canelones, bautice solemnemente a un parvulo ... por nombre HILARIO, hijo legitimo de MAN[uel Zeferino Barros] natural de PERNAMBUCO, y de JUSTA LLERENA ... padrinos PEDRO ANTONIO LLERENA y VICENTA VEGA.' SO PEDRO ANTONIO AND VICENTA VEGA STOOD AT A SECOND NEPHEW'S FONT. They are the godparents at the Aguada in 1852 for Avelino's son as well - two acts, seventeen years apart, the same couple standing for two different brothers' and sisters' children. THE BIRTH DATE IS NOT SETTLED AND THE COLUMN CARRIES ONLY THE YEAR. The index says 16 March 1835. The act's own phrase runs 'nacido a los ...' and this archive cannot read the word that follows at the resolution available; it appears to end 'del mismo', which would put the birth in APRIL and not March. Read at four times magnification before being left unresolved. A further reason for caution: 16 MARCH has now appeared as an indexed birth for three children of this family - Pedro Alberto Ysabelino 1852 and Andres 1857, both confirmed on their plates, and this one, which is not. TEST: the DeepZoom tiles of film 008166571 image 01993 at a level above dist.jpg.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Catedral Nuestra Senora de Guadalupe, Canelones - baptism of 19 April 1835, entry 46 - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 008166571 image 01993; index ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:689G-2W33" rel="noopener">1:1:689G-2W33</a>, image ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS2Z-W9J7-Z" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-CS2Z-W9J7-Z</a>

Juan Facundo Frutuoso BARROS Lerena
Unplaced		1831-11-27 · Cordon, Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo / Canelones
Grandson of Sancho Llerena. Born 27 November 1831 and baptised at the Cordon on 13 December. HIS GODPARENTS WERE DON FRUCTUOSO RIVERA, FIRST CONSTITUTIONAL PRESIDENT OF URUGUAY, AND HIS WIFE DONA BERNARDINA FRAGOSO - and he is named FRUTUOSO for the president. HIS DEATH IS FOUND, 21 September 2026: 3 MAY 1915 AT MONTEVIDEO, AGED EIGHTY-THREE, registered the next day, certificate 261. THE ARITHMETIC IS EXACT: a man born 27 November 1831 is eighty-three on 3 May 1915. The registration names his mother JUSTA LERENA and his father "MANUEL C BARROS", which is the Manuel Zeferino Barros of Pernambuco this archive already had - the C is for Ceferino. AND IT NAMES A WIFE THIS ARCHIVE DID NOT HAVE: EMILIA ANTELO. Found by a parent-pair query on his mother rather than on him. Index-level; the act is unopened. Nuestra Senora del Carmen la Mayor, Cordon, Montevideo, baptism of 13 Dec 1831 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-CS9Z-2" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-CS9Z-2</a> - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713388 image 00726, folio 133
Julia BASSANI
Unplaced		1864 · Italian or Austrian - THE TWO ACTS DISAGREE · labores de su casa · found in Montevideo, later Buenos Aires
Mother of Hector Narciso (1886) and Emilia Alberta (1888), both born at her own address, calle Ybirini N 321, Montevideo, while Alejandro M. Lerena was living at calle San Jose N 17. She is soltera on both acts and he is viudo, so the children are registered as hijos de los declarantes and no marriage is stated. HER NATIONALITY IS GIVEN TWO DIFFERENT WAYS, IN FRONT OF HER, SIXTEEN MONTHS APART: "italiana" in 1886 and "austriaca" in 1888. Aged 22 and then 24, which is consistent. The Austrian Empire held Italian-speaking lands until 1918, so a Trieste or Trentino subject could truthfully be described either way and the two need not conflict - but they are not the same claim and this archive records both rather than picking. Indexed elsewhere as Basani, Bossani, Barsani and Balani.		record ↗
Uruguay, Civil Registration 1879-2020 - birth act N 424, Montevideo, 6 November 1886; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:VR4Z-DDT" rel="noopener">1:1:VR4Z-DDT</a> - IMAGE READ 22 September 2026; film 004596830 image 01806; and Uruguay, Civil Registration 1879-2020 - birth act Num 108, Montevideo, March 1888; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:VR4Z-VVB" rel="noopener">1:1:VR4Z-VVB</a> - IMAGE READ 22 September 2026; film 004596830 image 02145

Uruguay Baptisms 1750-1900 / Catholic Church Records 1726-2000 / Civil Registration, read 13 Sep 2026
Juana BENTENO Correa
Unplaced		1789-06-23 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE CHILD SANCHO LLERENA HELD AT THE FONT, and this archive had her parents and her godparents without ever entering her. Born 23 June 1789 and baptised at the Iglesia Matriz seven days later, daughter of Nicolas Benteno, indio natural de la ciudad del Tucuman, and Leonarda Correa of Buenos Aires. NOT A RELATIVE. She is here because this is the earliest act in the archive that names Sancho, and because a godchild is a relationship the records of 1789 took seriously enough to warn the godparents about - 'a quienes adverti la COGNACION ESPIRITUAL'. Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo - baptism of 30 June 1789 - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713375 image 00551; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X74C-2" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X74C-2</a>
Nicolas BENTENO
Unplaced		Tucuman · of Tucuman · found in Montevideo
NOT A RELATIVE - recorded because of who stood at his daughter's font. "INDIO NATURAL DE LA CIUDAD DEL TUCUMAN", living at Montevideo, married to Leonarda Correa of Buenos Aires. At the baptism of their daughter JUANA on 30 June 1789 the godfather was SANCHO LLERENA - the earliest record of Sancho anywhere in this archive, fifteen years before Canelones and two years before his own marriage. It is the second time he is found sponsoring at the margins of colonial society; in 1804 he stood godfather to an enslaved man. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026. Film 007713375 image 00551, Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, 30 June 1789 - and this is the EARLIEST record of Sancho in this archive. The act: 'En TREINTA de [Junio] de mil setecientos ochenta y nueve ... bautice solemnemente en ella a JUANA que nacio el VEINTE Y TRES DEL CORRIENTE, hija legitima de NICOLAS BENTENO, INDIO NATURAL DE LA CIUDAD DEL TUCUMAN, y de LEONARDA CORREA de BUENOS AIRES, avecindados en los propios de esta Ciudad ... fueron padrinos SANCHO LLERENA y JULIANA JOSEFA [Cornejo], a quienes adverti la cognacion espiritual ... Siendo testigo AGUSTIN DONCEL.' SANCHO IS SPELLED LLERENA HERE, WITH THE DOUBLE L, IN 1789 - which is how the 1811 and 1831 acts spell him and not how 1837, 1858 or 1806 do. Every word of this row survives the plate: 'INDIO NATURAL DE LA CIUDAD DEL TUCUMAN', his wife 'LEONARDA CORREA de BUENOS AIRES', and the two of them 'AVECINDADOS EN LOS PROPIOS DE ESTA CIUDAD'. Adela CAMIGLIA
Unplaced		found in Mendoza
m. Carlos Lerena; mother of Avelino Carlos Lerena. NOT THE SAME CARLOS LERENA AS ROSA MARIA EPPENS'S HUSBAND - checked 14 September 2026. A consistency check flagged that two rows here each say 'm. Carlos Lerena'. They are two different men. Hers is at MENDOZA and is the father of Avelino Carlos Lerena; the other is the URUGUAYAN CARLOS b.1885 who landed at Buenos Aires in 1926 travelling with Rosa M. E. Lerena herself. Recorded because nothing else in either row said so, and a later reader merging them would invent a marriage. The Mendoza connection is worth its own look: a CARLOS MARTINEZ LERENA appears in Mendoza parish records as a child of Julian Martinez and Carolina Lerena. Argentina Mendoza Catholic Church Records, 1904
Custodio CARDOZO (the elder, of the 1911 diagram)
Unplaced		Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE APEX OF THE SECOND SCHEMA - father of FLORINDA and ELISEO CARDOZO, and so the grandfather of BOTH the bride and the groom of 1911. He is the reason the petition needed two diagrams instead of one: his two children married two Lerena siblings, and his two grandchildren then wanted to marry each other. THE GROOM IS NAMED FOR HIM: CUSTODIO LERENA. Like his opposite number Bernabe Lerena the elder, he exists in this archive solely because a priest drew a circle and wrote a name beside it, and he was not entered until the plate was read on 21 September 2026.		no public link
Named at the apex of one of the two kinship diagrams in the Lerena-Cardozo dispensation of 1911 - &#39;Esquema explicativo de los grados de parentesco&#39; - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713362 image 02473; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-GS3N-1" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-GS3N-1</a>
Eliseo CARDOZO
Unplaced		Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Married MARIA LERENA; father of Ema Cardozo. Brother of Florinda Cardozo, who married Maria's brother Bernabe Lerena. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713362 image 02473 - and the folio is not prose but 'Esquema explicativo de los grados de parentesco', TWO KINSHIP DIAGRAMS the priest drew himself to prove the impediment. 'Eliseo Cardozo casado con Maria Lerena' - on the plate. His father is named at the apex of the second schema: CUSTODIO CARDOZO.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Godmother with Sancho Llerena at the Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, 30 June 1789. Nothing else in this archive names her. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026. Film 007713375 image 00551, Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, 30 June 1789 - and this is the EARLIEST record of Sancho in this archive. The act: 'En TREINTA de [Junio] de mil setecientos ochenta y nueve ... bautice solemnemente en ella a JUANA que nacio el VEINTE Y TRES DEL CORRIENTE, hija legitima de NICOLAS BENTENO, INDIO NATURAL DE LA CIUDAD DEL TUCUMAN, y de LEONARDA CORREA de BUENOS AIRES, avecindados en los propios de esta Ciudad ... fueron padrinos SANCHO LLERENA y JULIANA JOSEFA [Cornejo], a quienes adverti la cognacion espiritual ... Siendo testigo AGUSTIN DONCEL.' SANCHO IS SPELLED LLERENA HERE, WITH THE DOUBLE L, IN 1789 - which is how the 1811 and 1831 acts spell him and not how 1837, 1858 or 1806 do. The act gives her as 'JULIANA JOSEFA' and the surname runs past the column edge, so CORNEJO comes from the transcript and not from the part of the line this archive has read. It is not disputed; it is simply not yet confirmed on the plate.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Agustin DONCEL (witness, 1789)
Unplaced		found in Montevideo
Witness at the baptism of 30 June 1789 where Sancho Llerena stood godfather: 'Siendo testigo AGUSTIN DONCEL'. Sancho's eldest son was christened JUAN AGUSTIN about two years later. This family names children for the people at the font five times over in the acts read here - but every one of those is a GODPARENT, and this man is a WITNESS, which is a weaker place to stand. Recorded as a coincidence worth seeing and not as a source for the name. The surname is read DONCEL and could be DONCE.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Dona Maria FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS (wife of Sancho)
Unplaced		Asturias, Spain · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Wife of Sancho Llerena and mother of Don Luis Lerena - which is where the "y Fernandez" in his name comes from. She stood godmother at her grandson Luis Eugenio's baptism in 1846. HER FULL NAME IS ON AN ACT, 14 September 2026 - and it vindicates a tree. Her daughter's marriage of 3 November 1830 names the bride "hija legitima de Sancho Llerena y de MARIA FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS". This archive has carried "Maria Josefa Fernandez Tresguerras" since its first week FROM A PUBLISHED TREE, marked secondary, with the matronymic unverified. The tree was right, and right about something no index anywhere gives. Which raises the standing of that same tree's other unopened claim - that she married Sancho at CANELONES on 8 JANUARY 1791. SHE IS ALSO THE ONE FIXED POINT IN HER HUSBAND'S IDENTITY: across fourteen records his forename alternates between Sancho and Francisco, and every single one of them names HER. The Geneanet trees give her more fully as MARIA JOSEFA FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS, and two of them add TERESA. Today's marriage act says only "Maria Fernandez Tresguerras" - but since that act has just vindicated the trees on TRESGUERRAS itself, the extra forenames are worth more than they were yesterday. Still tree-only, and not adopted. HER BIRTHPLACE AND HER PARENTS ARE NOW ON AN ACT, 15 September 2026. The 1811 baptism of her son Luis calls her "Dona MARIA FERNANDEZ natural de ASTURIAS" and names "abuelos maternos RODRIGO FERNANDEZ y ROSA FERNANDEZ" ASTURIAS confirms the Operativo Patagonia reading from a Uruguayan document - her family were of the 1778-1781 convoy, 41 Asturian founder families of which seven came from MORCIN, the concejo the trees give as her birthplace. And RODRIGO FERNANDEZ is the same man the 1812 padron records living in Sancho's household, aged 63, "asturiano" - so the household, the act and the trees agree on him from three directions. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT since 14 September. Film 007713375 image 02410, Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, the baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 February 1811 - the only record in this archive that names Sancho's own parents. 'y de D.a MARIA [Fernandez] nat.l de ASTURIAS. ... id. mat.s RODRIGO FERNANDEZ y ROSA FERNANDEZ' - read off the plate. Her parents are named on this act and are now entered as people, which they had not been since the act was found on 14 September. HER BIRTHPLACE IN THIS REGISTER SAID 'Montevideo' UNTIL 21 SEPTEMBER 2026, AND THE ARCHIVE'S OWN WORKING LOG HAD SAID OTHERWISE FOR A WEEK. data/1811-luis-baptism-act.tsv recorded on 14 September that the act calls her 'natural de ASTURIAS', and the register was never changed. SO BOTH HALVES OF SANCHO'S HOUSEHOLD WERE SPANISH IMMIGRANTS, FROM OPPOSITE ENDS OF SPAIN - he from Andalusia, she from Asturias, meeting at Montevideo. That is a different family story from the one a Montevideo birthplace tells, and it is the one the earliest act gives. AND IT SHARPENS A CONTRADICTION FOUND THIS MORNING: the 1852 baptism of her granddaughter Josefina at the Union calls the paternal grandparents 'orientales'. An 1852 priest, describing two people who had been dead for decades, against the act of 1811 in which their own son is baptised. The 1811 act wins, and the 1852 'orientales' is recorded as a mistake of the record, not of this archive.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Named as paternal grandmother in the 1850 act, and as godmother in 1846 - IMAGE READ; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-ZSZQ-J" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-ZSZQ-J</a>; and her daughter Justa Pastora&#39;s marriage act, Cordon, 3 Nov 1830 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ, ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-B3WZ-8" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-B3WZ-8</a>; Montevideo, Iglesia Matriz, baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 Feb 1811 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ, ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X7F6-M" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X7F6-M</a> - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713375 image 02410
Rodrigo FERNANDEZ (father of Maria)
Unplaced		c.1749 · Asturias, Spain · Spanish · found in Montevideo
FATHER OF MARIA FERNANDEZ AND FATHER-IN-LAW OF SANCHO - named on the 1811 act and entered on 21 September 2026, a week after the act was read. 'id. mat.s RODRIGO FERNANDEZ y ROSA FERNANDEZ'. He is one of the two oldest people in this register alongside Felix Llerena and Rosalia Aguilar, and like them he is known from a single line naming him as a grandparent. HE HAD BEEN SITTING IN A WORKING LOG SINCE 14 SEPTEMBER AND WAS NEVER MADE A PERSON - which is this archive's most persistent fault, and the reason it now has a gate for it. Nothing here gives his dates, and Asturias is inferred from his daughter's stated birthplace, not stated of him. AND THE 'PRESUMED' IN THE BIRTHPLACE COLUMN CAME OFF WITHIN THE HOUR. This archive already held him, without ever making him a person: data/padron-1812-image.tsv records that the household of 1812 ends 'RODRIGO FERNANDEZ ... at 63, ASTURIANO ... the last name before the next bracket'. So Asturias is ATTESTED, not inferred, and an age of 63 in 1812 puts his birth about 1749. TWO INDEPENDENT DOCUMENTS OF THE SAME YEAR: the baptism of February 1811 names him as the child's maternal grandfather, and the padron of September 1812 puts him under the same roof. SANCHO'S FATHER-IN-LAW WAS LIVING IN THE HOUSE - an Asturian of sixty-three, in a household of an Andalusian, his Asturian daughter, their eight children and four enslaved people. Nothing here says when he came, or whether he came with her.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Montevideo, Iglesia Matriz, baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 Feb 1811 - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713375 image 02410; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X7F6-M" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-X7F6-M</a>; and the Padron de Montevideo de 1812, calle San Juan No 60, where he closes Sancho&#39;s household at 63, ASTURIANO - ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-99ZR-NKNR" rel="noopener">3:1:3QS7-99ZR-NKNR</a>
Rosa FERNANDEZ (mother of Maria)
Unplaced		Asturias, Spain (presumed - her daughter is 'natural de Asturias') · Spanish · found in Montevideo
MOTHER OF MARIA FERNANDEZ, named on the 1811 act - and she carries the SAME SURNAME AS HER HUSBAND. 'id. mat.s Rodrigo Fernandez y ROSA FERNANDEZ'. That is worth a sentence rather than a shrug: FERNANDEZ is among the commonest surnames in Spain, so a couple both called Fernandez needs no explanation at all - but it is also exactly what a priest writes when he knows one surname and assumes the other. Nothing here distinguishes the two readings. Entered as the act gives her. AND SHE IS NOT IN THE HOUSE. The padron of 1812 ends Sancho's household with her husband RODRIGO FERNANDEZ at 63 and does not name her. That is not evidence she was dead - the padron lists who was in the house on the day - but it is the only thing this archive can say about her after 1811. Hermenegilda Carolina GAVAZZO (de LERENA)
Unplaced		c.1862 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Married Alfredo Lerena Traibel at the Catedral Basilica de Montevideo, 3 February 1892, aged thirty. Daughter of Don Geronimo Gavarro and Dona Carolina Juarez. She and her husband were COUSINS - the act dispenses an impediment of the third degree of consanguinity with atingencia to the second. THE SURNAME IS CORRECTED OFF THE IMAGE, 21 September 2026. This archive held it from a MACHINE FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT of the 1892 marriage act and never opened the page. The page was opened - film 007713383 image 02601, folio 340 - and the act reads 'hija legitima de Don GERONIMO GAVAZZO y Dona CAROLINA SUAREZ'. GAVAZZO, not Gavarro; SUAREZ, not Juarez. It is corroborated independently by Hermenegilda's own Montevideo death registration of 20 June 1940, certificate 632, read off film 008166249 image 01900, which writes her HERMENEGILDA GAVAZZO DE LERENA and her parents Geronimo Gavazzo and Carolina Suarez. Two images, forty-eight years apart, against one machine transcript. AND SHE HAS A DEATH, found the same day: 20 JUNE 1940, at MONTEVIDEO. The act says she died at R. Masini 3273, 'de estado VIUDA', 'a consecuencia de FRACTURA DEL CUELLO DEL FEMUR' - a broken hip, which for a woman of about seventy-eight in 1940 is usually the end. Certificate 632, registered by Jose M. Reyes Corvalan. SO ALFREDO JUSTINIANO DIED BEFORE 20 JUNE 1940, which is the first thing this archive has ever known about the end of his life. She was a widow, and the marriage had lasted at least into its fifth decade.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Dona Juliana Joaquina GONZALEZ
Unplaced		found in Montevideo
Mother of Julia Juanico, and wife of Dr Candido Juanico. Published genealogy gives Candido's wife as SIXTA Lenguas Gonzalez, married 1857 - too late for a daughter marrying in 1864, so Juliana is an earlier wife. That is inference from dates. A THIRD ACT NOW NAMES HER, 13 September 2026: the baptism of her grandson Candido Jaime, San Francisco de Asis act No. 1720 of 6 July 1886, reads "maternos: Don Candido y Dona Juliana Gonzalez". Twenty-two years after the marriage act, the same parish still gives Julia's mother as Juliana Gonzalez. The published genealogy's SIXTA Lenguas Gonzalez is not what the registers say. AND THE TWO-WIVES INFERENCE IS CONFIRMED. Searching Candido Juanico as a father returns two distinct sets of children: Julia, by Juliana Joaquina Gonzalez, born 1842; and Sara, Zara Sista Emirena and Celia, all JUANICO LENGUAS, born 1866-1869 to SIXTA LENGUAS. A full generation apart. The published genealogy was right about the 1857 marriage and wrong to attach it to Julia.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Juan Carlos GONZALEZ LERENA (m. 1920s, Montevideo)
Excluded		Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
CHECKED AND EXCLUDED, 15 September 2026 - he is the only 'Juan Carlos Lerena' in FamilySearch's entire full-text corpus apart from a 2001 death record. The Vicar General "dispenso a Don Juan Carlos Gonzalez Lerena las tres conciliares proclamas en el matrimonio que intenta contraer con ... Concepcion Petrona Urbano". LERENA IS HIS SECOND SURNAME - his mother's - not his father's. That is the same shape as the only Juan Carlos Lerena in all of Geneanet, a "del Campo Lerena". Pablo Armando's father must carry LERENA as the PATERNAL surname and must have been born by about the 1850s to father a son in 1882. This man is marrying in the 1920s. Recorded so that the next reader who runs this search does not have to spend an hour excluding him. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, AND THE COMPOUND SURNAME IS REALLY THERE. Film 007713367 image 02860, folio 391, entry No. 14610: 'El OCHO DE NOVIEMBRE de mil novecientos VEINTE Y DOS, el Rmo. Sr. Vicario General ... en uso de sus facultades dispensó a Don JUAN CARLOS GONZALEZ LERENA las tres conciliares proclamas en el matrimonio que intenta contraer con ...'. So the date is 8 NOVEMBER 1922, not merely 'the 1920s', and the register of Montevideo dispensations numbers it 14610. THE BRIDE'S NAME IS ONLY PARTLY LEGIBLE and is not written down here as a reading: the words run '...cepcion Petrona' and then a surname this archive cannot settle between URBANO and a similar hand. IT IS NOT GUESSED. WHY THIS MAN IS IN THIS REGISTER AT ALL: he is a JUAN CARLOS, which is the forename this whole archive turns on - Pablo Armando's father is named Juan Carlos Lerena on the 1908 enrolment card. THIS IS NOT HIM AND WAS NEVER CLAIMED TO BE: this man carries GONZALEZ as his paternal surname and LERENA as his mother's, and he is marrying at Montevideo in 1922, forty years after Pablo Armando was born at Rosario. He is kept because a Juan Carlos with LERENA anywhere in his name is worth being able to rule out on the record rather than meeting again in five years' time. 1812 padron, calle San Juan No 60; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QS7-99ZR-NKNR" rel="noopener">3:1:3QS7-99ZR-NKNR</a>
JUANA (enslaved in Sancho Llerena's household)
Connected		found in Montevideo, calle San Juan No 60
A WOMAN THIS FAMILY HELD IN SLAVERY, NAMED HERE FOR THE FIRST TIME. The padron of 1812 records four enslaved people in Sancho Llerena's household and gives none of them a name. The baptism of her daughter, six weeks later in the same year, names her: JUANA. Nothing else is known about her - no age, no origin, no surname, no later record. This row exists so that the archive carries her name rather than a number. See also her daughter MARIA MERCEDES. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713375 image 02628, folio 136, Montevideo Catedral - the baptism of 26 September 1812. HER NAME SURVIVES THE CHECK, and that is the whole reason this plate was opened. The act reads 'que nacio antes de ayer, hija de JUANA, negra esclava de D.n SANCHO LERENA y de D.a MARIA FERNANDEZ'. A machine is worst at proper nouns, and this woman's name is the only thing any record gives her - so it was the one that most needed a human to read it. It is JUANA, in the priest's own hand. The act also states that she was held by BOTH Sancho and Maria Fernandez, jointly - which the transcript rendered the same way and which the padron of the same month confirms by putting her inside that household. Unplaced		c.1812 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · abogado, magistrado, legislador · found in Montevideo
Father of Julia Juanico. Son of Francisco Juanico and Juliana Texeira. Stood as witness at his daughter's wedding in December 1864. HE MARRIED TWICE. Juliana Joaquina Gonzalez first - Julia, b.1842, is hers - and SIXTA LENGUAS second, by whom Sara, Zara Sista Emirena and Celia Juanico Lenguas, born 1866-1869. Candido Juanico, Oriental, edad 72 anos, viudo, ABOGADO" - so born about 1812, and a lawyer, which is what this archive's "Dr." has always rested on. Dr. D. Damaso LARRANAGA (Vicar, 1833)
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · Cura y Vicario · found in Montevideo
Named on the plate as the Cura y Vicario whose licence allowed the baptism of 19 October 1833: 'con licencia del Cura y Vicario D.r D. DAMASO LARRANAGA'. DAMASO ANTONIO LARRANAGA is one of the founding figures of Uruguay - first Vicar Apostolic of Montevideo, naturalist, and founder of the National Library. NO RELATIONSHIP TO THIS FAMILY IS CLAIMED AND NONE EXISTS: he is named because he held the office, as he is on thousands of Montevideo acts of these years. He is entered for the same reason the LAVALLEJAS, the RIVERAS, the ORIBES and GENERAL MAGGESI are: this archive records who was in the room, and in a small city in the 1830s the room kept containing the men in the history books. The fourth such name checked against a plate today, and the fourth to be really there.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Maria Ines LARRIERA
Unplaced		Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Wife of Alberto Lerena (Pedro Alberto Ysabelino, 1852-1921), named at his death. Nothing else in this archive names her. 'NOTHING ELSE IN THIS ARCHIVE NAMES HER' LASTED THREE HOURS. Eight children born in the Departamento de San Jose between 1879 and 1898 name her as their mother, and one of them - ZELMIRA CARLOTA - has been in this register since 14 September with her name on it, unrecognised. A VICTORIA CROSADE LARRIERA stood godmother at her son Carlos Ambrosio Florencio's baptism in 1889, presumably a sister. HER HUSBAND'S DEATH ACT WAS READ OFF THE IMAGE THE SAME DAY: 'era casado con MARIA INES LARRIERA, de cuya union quedan NUEVE HIJOS'. She outlived him - the act does not call her fallecida where it calls both his parents so - and she had borne nine children, one more than the civil index surfaces.		no public link

Justiniano LAVALLEJA
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Godfather to Alfredo JUSTINIANO Lerena Traibel at San Francisco de Asis on 12 March 1858, and the man the child is named for. LAVALLEJA is one of the great names of Uruguayan independence - Juan Antonio Lavalleja led the Treinta y Tres Orientales in 1825. NO RELATIONSHIP TO HIM IS CLAIMED HERE: Lavalleja is a surname, not a proof. What the record shows is that this family stood at fonts with Lavallejas, as it had with a RIVERA in 1831 and married an ORIBE in 1836. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713686 image 02847, folio 176, San Francisco de Asis - the two acts of 12 March 1858 that attach the Lerena Traibel branch to Sancho. EVERY NAME ON THEM SURVIVES THE CHECK, word for word. THIS WAS THE NAME MOST AT RISK AND IT HOLDS. A machine that meets a famous Uruguayan surname will write it whether or not the page says so - that is exactly how FRUCTUOSO RIVERA got printed over the 1831 act's own FRUTUSO RIBERA. Here the page says 'JUSTINIANO LAVALLEJA', and it says it in a hand that also spells LERENA and TRAIBEL correctly on the same line.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Unplaced		1807 · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE FURTHEST BACK THE LENGUAS LINE NOW GOES, reached 14 September 2026. Father of General Don Pedro Lenguas, and so great-grandfather of both Luis Eugenio and Gilberto Lerena. Named with his wife Juana Grandal in his granddaughter Sixta's death act. Dona Justiniana LENGUAS Gonzalez
Unplaced		c.1822 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Mother of the LERENA LENGUAS, and named as such in TWO acts read here: her son Luis Eugenio's marriage of 19 December 1864, at which she was also a witness, and her granddaughter Laura Adela's baptism of 1869, which calls her one of the "abuelos paternos". Her sister SIXTA LENGUAS GONZALEZ married Dr Candido Juanico - so the Lenguas married into both houses, and Sixta stood godmother to Laura Adela in 1869. That she was the second wife of Luis Lerena y Fernandez, and a daughter of Gen. Pedro Lenguas Grandal, is SECONDARY. SHE WAS ALIVE ON 24 DECEMBER 1901, AGED 79, and in the house where her son died. His death act No. 216 records her "de setenta y nueve anos, de profesion labores, domiciliada accidentalmente en la calle Suarez No. 25" - which puts her birth about 1822 and has her outliving Luis Eugenio. AND BY 1908 SHE WAS LIVING IN ARGENTINA, AGED 87. Her grandson Jose Julio's birth registration of that year describes her "de ochenta y siete anos, oriental, viuda, labores, RESIDENTE EN LA REPUBLICA ARGENTINA". She was 79 and in Montevideo when her son died in 1901; seven years later the matriarch of this family had crossed the river - and her son GILBERTO had moved to Buenos Aires between 1887 and 1889. HER OWN PARENTS ARE NOW IN AN ACT: "Maternos: el GENERAL D. PEDRO LENGUAS y Carmen Gonzalez". And that settles a question this archive had never been able to answer: SIXTA LENGUAS, the second wife of Dr Candido Juanico, has the very same parents on her 1884 death registration - so JUSTINIANA AND SIXTA ARE SISTERS. When Luis Eugenio married Julia Juanico in December 1864, the groom's maternal AUNT was already the bride's STEPMOTHER. The families had been joined since 1857. HER MARRIAGE ACT, 3 MARCH 1842: "Justiniana Lenguas, natural de la Ciudad de Montevideo, de VEINTE anos de edad, hija legitima del Sr. GRAL. Lenguas y Dona Carmen Gonzalez." Twenty years old, so born about 1822 - agreeing exactly with her stated 79 in 1901 and 87 in 1908. She was her husband's SECOND wife; he was a widower of a Dona Carolina. AND SHE WAS ALREADY A WIDOW IN 1895, aged 73, living in Gilberto's Buenos Aires house - "Lenguas de L. Justiniana, m, 73, VIUDA, R. Oriental" on the schedule of Seccion 21, Manzana 15. So DON LUIS LERENA WAS DEAD BY 1895, which closes his death window to 1850-1895. She was in Argentina thirteen years earlier than the 1908 evidence this archive had. Dona Sixta LENGUAS Gonzalez (2nd wife of Candido Juanico)
Unplaced		c.1837 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE SISTER OF JUSTINIANA LENGUAS - established 13 September 2026. Her death registration of 17 January 1884 names her parents as "Pedro Lenguas, Carmen Gonzalez" and her husband as "Candido Juanico"; the 1846 baptism acts name Justiniana's parents as the same couple. So the second wife of Julia Juanico's father was the sister of Luis Eugenio's mother, and the two families were joined in 1857 - seven years before the marriage this archive has treated as the join. Married Candido Juanico in 1857 per a published genealogy; the mother of Sara, Zara Sista Emirena and Celia Juanico Lenguas, born 1866-1869. She is NOT the mother of Julia, who was born in 1842 to Juliana Joaquina Gonzalez - an inference this archive made from the dates before it could prove it, and then proved. General Don Pedro LENGUAS
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Father of JUSTINIANA and of SIXTA Lenguas, and called "el GENERAL" in his granddaughter's baptism act. Through Justiniana he is great-grandfather to Gilberto Lerena's children; through Sixta he is father-in-law to Dr Candido Juanico. He also stood godfather at his granddaughter's christening. Pedro" as a forename - "Geraldo Dios Lenguas" - which is how a military rank turns into a christian name and a searchable person who never existed. Abelino LERENA (godfather, 1833)
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A LERENA MAN OF SANCHO'S OWN GENERATION OR THE NEXT, found 14 September 2026. He stood godfather with Dominga Lerena at the Catedral in October 1833 to a child of Justa Pastora Lerena - so almost certainly her brother or cousin. The transcript renders him "Abelino Serena", one more instance of the S-for-L this archive has met before. AVELINO is also the forename attached to the LERENA TRAIBEL branch elsewhere in this archive, which is worth testing rather than assuming. THE AGE NOW FITS, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS PROOF. Avelino Lerena Fernandez, Sancho's son, is now dated to a birth of 1815 - so he was EIGHTEEN in October 1833. Young for a godfather and entirely possible, and he would have been standing for his own sister's child. STILL KEPT AS A SEPARATE ROW until a record joins them, for the reason the two Arturos exist in this archive. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, AND THE PLATE SAYS LERENA, NOT SERENA. So the S-for-L in this row was the MACHINE'S, not the record's - the third time today an assertion about this family's spelling turned out to be a transcript artefact rather than a scribe's. The act reads 'Padrinos ABELINO LERENA y DOMINGA LERENA'. AND THE CO-GODPARENT MATTERS MORE THAN THE SPELLING. He did not stand alone: he stood with DOMINGA LERENA, and Dominga Josefa Llerena Fernandez is a DOCUMENTED daughter of Sancho, baptised at Canelones on 15 May 1804. So the two at this font are a documented Sancho daughter and this man, at the baptism of a third Sancho daughter's child. That is corroboration from 1833 for a parentage the 1858 acts settled - twenty-five years earlier and from a different parish. It is still not proof: a cousin can stand beside a sister. Agustina Ramona LERENA Fernandez
Connected		1795-08-27 · Canelones ("el Canelon") · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo, calle San Juan No 60
ONE OF SANCHO'S SIX CHILDREN LISTED IN THE 1812 PADRON, and the first time any of them has been entered here. The household at calle San Juan No 60 held Sancho "andaluz" 45, Maria Fernandez 36, and six children all "naturales del CANELON" - Juan Agustin 21, Pedro Antonio 19, Agustina 17, Fidel 12, Justa 10 and Dominga 7 - together with Sancho's father-in-law RODRIGO FERNANDEZ, 63, asturiano, and four enslaved people. That all six were born at Canelones is the best support the 1791 marriage venue has, and it comes from a census rather than from a tree. NOT AN ACT: this row rests on a published transcription of a census, reported at second hand through a family tree. The act has not been opened here. Aged 17 in the 1812 padron, so born about 1795, which the tree gives exactly. She is the second half of the 1812 godparent pair - "Dona AGUSTINA PERENA" - standing with her elder brother Juan Agustin at a Fernandez grandchild's baptism. Her forename repeats his second, which is the ordinary Spanish habit of naming siblings from one saint. HER ACT IS FOUND, 15 September 2026, one day after she was entered here from a census age and a tree. Canelones, 30 August 1795: "bautice puse el Santo Oleo y Crisma a AGUSTINA RAMONA de tres dias nacida, hija legitima de Don Sancho Lerena y de Dona Maria Fernandez vecinos de la Villa". Born 27 August 1795, and her second forename is RAMONA. Her godfather was DON FIDEL REY of Montevideo - which is where her younger brother FIDEL got a forename that appears nowhere else in this family. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, AND SHE GAINS A SECOND FORENAME. Film 008166571 image 01017: 'a TREINTA DIAS DEL MES DE AGOSTO ... yo el Cura y Vic.o de esta Parroquia de los CANELONES ... bautize, puse el Santo Oleo y Crisma a AGUSTINA RAMONA, DE TRES DIAS NACIDA, hija legitima de D.n SANCHO LERENA y de D.a Maria Fernandez, VECINOS DE LA VILLA. Fueron sus padrinos D.n FIDEL REY, vecino de MONTEVIDEO, y D.a JOSEFA ... de la misma Villa.' 'DE TRES DIAS NACIDA' CONFIRMS THE BIRTH OF 27 AUGUST 1795 that this register carried from a tree and a padron. AND THE GODFATHER EXPLAINS A NAME THIS FAMILY USES: FIDEL. Sancho's next son but one was christened FIDEL, about 1799 - four years after Don Fidel Rey stood at this font. This family names children for whoever stands at the font, five times over in the acts read here, so the likeliest source of the forename FIDEL in the Lerena is this man. IT IS AN INFERENCE FROM A PATTERN, NOT FROM A RECORD, and it does not touch the separate question of whether the FIDEL LERENA who married Maria Ruedas is Sancho's son. The act also carries a scribal self-correction - the teniente cura is written 'D.r Christoval Munoz QUIERO DECIR ...' - and the Cura y Vicario signs JOSE MANUEL DE ROO, the same hand that signs Jose Loreto's act eleven years later. Alejandro Joaquin Narciso LERENA Villardemoros

Born 29 October 1872 and christened at the Iglesia del Cordon on 29 May 1873, son of Alejandro Lerena and Elisa Villardemoros. His godparents are the interesting part: JOAQUIN REGUSA Y GARCIA and ANGELICA VILLADEMOROS. Uruguay, registros parroquiales 1726-2000 - christening of Alejandro Joaquin Narciso Lerena, Cordon, Montevideo, 29 May 1873; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:6D3Z-WTXR" rel="noopener">1:1:6D3Z-WTXR</a>; index read 20 September 2026
Alejandro LERENA (m. Elisa VILLARDEMOROS)
Unplaced · high interest		Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A SECOND LERENA MARRIED A SECOND VILLADEMOROS, and the two marriages are probably one alliance. He and ELISA VILLARDEMOROS had a son christened at the Cordon on 29 May 1873. In the same decade AVELINO EDUARDO LERENA - documented as a son of Avelino Lerena and Ambrosia Traibel - married CELEDONIA VILLADEMOROS. THREE THINGS TIE THE TWO BAPTISMS TOGETHER: the surname on both mothers; a JOAQUIN standing godfather at each, indexed "Joaquin Requena" on one and "Joaquin Regusa y Garcia" on the other, which is one name and one machine mangling it; and the godmother here is ANGELICA VILLADEMOROS, while Celedonia's mother is ANGELICA MENDEZ - a daughter named for her. THE INFERENCE: two Lerena brothers married two Villademoros sisters, which is the same shape as the two Lerena siblings who married two Cardozo siblings and produced the 1911 dispensation. WHAT IS NOT DOCUMENTED: this act does NOT name his own parents, so he is NOT joined to Alejandro Lerena Traibel here, though the age fits - that man was born 1843 or 1845 and would be twenty-seven or twenty-nine. THE TEST: the two marriage acts, or any record naming this Alejandro's father. AND THE TEST NAMED ABOVE WAS RUN THE SAME EVENING AND CAME BACK EMPTY. LERENA with spouse surname VILLADEMOROS, Uruguay: 6,352 records, and not one of them is a marriage act. Every hit is a parent entry on somebody else's baptism or civil registration. The control is the one that worked two hours earlier: the same query shape on LERENA with spouse JUANICO puts the marriage act of 19 December 1864 at RANK ONE. So the shape finds a marriage when there is one to find. NEITHER VILLADEMOROS MARRIAGE IS IN THE INDEXED RECORD, and the brothers-and-sisters reading stays an inference. It would be settled by the parish marriage books of the Cordon and Union, which have not been walked. THE NULL PUBLISHED AGAINST THIS ROW RESTS ON A CONTROL THAT DID NOT CONTROL FOR THE RIGHT RISK - noted 21 September 2026, and the null is NOT withdrawn. The finding was that LERENA with spouse surname VILLADEMOROS returns 6,352 records and not one marriage act, controlled by LERENA with spouse JUANICO, which put the 1864 marriage at rank one. JUANICO HAS ONE SPELLING AND VILLADEMOROS HAS AT LEAST THREE. This register already holds VILLADEMOROS, VILLARDEMOROS and, from a death registration read today, VILLADEMORO. And on the same day a parent-pair query on this surname returned a ZERO that a single deleted parameter turned into SEVEN. So the control proved the query SHAPE works; it did not prove that this SURNAME is reachable through it. The two marriage acts may be absent from the index, or they may be present under a spelling nobody asked for. Until the Cordon and Union marriage books are walked, the difference is not known - and that is a weaker claim than the one this archive published.		no public link
Uruguay, registros parroquiales 1726-2000 - christening of Alejandro Joaquin Narciso Lerena, Cordon, Montevideo, 29 May 1873; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:6D3Z-WTXR" rel="noopener">1:1:6D3Z-WTXR</a>; index read 20 September 2026
Alejandro LERENA TRAIBEL (formerly recorded as LERENA LENGUAS)
Unplaced		1843-05-29 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
CORRECTED 14 SEPTEMBER 2026, AND THE CORRECTION MOVES HIM BETWEEN BRANCHES. This archive recorded him on 13 September as "A SIXTH CHILD of Luis Lerena and Justiniana Lenguas", born 29 May 1845. BOTH HALVES WERE WRONG. The act reads: "bautizo solemnemente a ALEJANDRO, que nacio el veinte y nueve de Mayo del pasado ano CUARENTA Y TRES, hijo legitimo de AVELINO SERENA y AMBROSIA TRAIBEL. Paternos FRANCISCO y MARIA FERNANDEZ. Maternos JUAN e ISABEL CENTENO. Padrinos LUIS [L]ERENA y JUSTINIANA LENGUAS." He is a son of AVELINO, not of Luis - and Luis and Justiniana are his GODPARENTS, which is how the archive came to read them as his parents. The two brothers brought their children to the same font on the same morning and stood for each other's. So he is the ELDEST KNOWN CHILD OF AVELINO LERENA AND AMBROSIA TRAIBEL - eight years before Josefa and thirteen before Alfredo, who was born 2 October 1856 and not the 1858 this register carried until 20 September - and this row said he "pushes their marriage back before May 1843". THAT LAST CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN, 20 September 2026, AND SO IS THE CONFIDENCE IN HIS BIRTH YEAR. The page was re-read end to end and all three acts on it give the year in the identical words, "del pasado ano cuarenta y tres" - including one for a different couple. The phrase contradicts itself (these acts open "En siete de Enero de mil ochocientos cuarenta y seis", so "el pasado ano" is 1845), and it cannot be literally true, because the two children of Luis and Justiniana on this page would then be born five months apart. So the year here is a formula, not a statement, and nothing outside this page dates Alejandro at all. His birth is UNDETERMINED BETWEEN 29 MAY 1843 AND 29 MAY 1845, the born_est below is the earlier of the two and not a finding, and the safe floor for the marriage of Avelino and Ambrosia is before May 1845. See data/montevideo-1846-lerena-baptisms.tsv. AND HE IS A FOURTH ALEJANDRO. See data/the-three-alejandros.tsv: the enrolment cards of Cajon 985 name an ALEJANDRO MAXIMO LERENA, born Uruguay 1839, whose parents no record here names. Four years apart from this man. NOTHING POSITIVE LINKS THEM and they stay separate rows, but the candidate pool has changed and the table needs re-reading.		no public link
La Mauricia, Villa de la Union, Montevideo, baptised 7 Jan 1846 - IMAGE READ and FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-ZS62-J" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-ZS62-J</a>
Alejandro M. LERENA (with Julia BASSANI)
Unplaced · high interest		1844 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · escribano (notary) · found in Montevideo, later Buenos Aires
THE ALEJANDRO M. LERENA OF THE ARMY ENROLMENT CARDS, CAJON 985 - and the M. is on the acts, in the clerk's hand, not only in the index. He appeared in person twice: on 6 November 1886, "parecieron don Alejandro M. Lerena, de cuarenta y dos anos de edad, viudo, escribano, oriental, domiciliado en la calle San Jose N 17"; and again in March 1888, "comparecieron don Alejandro M. Lerena de 43 anos de edad de estado viudo de nacionalidad Oriental". TWO AGES SIXTEEN MONTHS APART, CONSISTENT WITH EACH OTHER, PUT HIS BIRTH BETWEEN 17 MARCH AND 6 NOVEMBER 1844. The tree's "born Uruguay 1839" is five years out on both acts and is a tree value, not a record. HE IS A WIDOWER IN 1886 while Julia Bassani is twenty-two and soltera, so there is an earlier marriage and an earlier death in Montevideo - and a marriage act names the groom's parents, which is now the shortest route to his parentage. THE 1888 ACT IS A PRINTED FORM WHOSE RULED LINE READS "Que es nieta por linea paterna de ______" AND THE LINE IS BLANK. So is the maternal one. The form asked this archive's question and nobody wrote the answer on it. NOT MERGED with Alejandro LERENA TRAIBEL (b. 1843 or 1845, son of Avelino) - the ages miss both of that man's candidate dates by one year, in opposite directions, which cannot choose between them. See data/the-three-alejandros.tsv. MAXIMO IS NOW ATTESTED ON A PLATE, 22 September 2026, and not only in the tree. His son Hector's marriage act of 25 February 1911 at San Carlos, Buenos Aires, names him in the priest's hand as "D. ALEJANDRO MAXIMO [Lerena], natural de la Rep. Oriental". So the bare "M." of the two Montevideo birth acts expands to MAXIMO, and the Alejandro M. Lerena of army Cajon 985 has the same expansion. He was still being named as a living father's name in Buenos Aires in 1911.		record ↗

Alfredo Justiniano LERENA Traibel
Unplaced		1856-10-02 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A SON OF AVELINO LERENA AND AMBROSIA TRAIBEL, and a brother of Josefa - found 14 September 2026. Married HERMENEGILDA CAROLINA GAVARRO at the Catedral Basilica de Montevideo on 3 February 1892, aged thirty-five. THE MARRIAGE NEEDED A DISPENSATION FOR CONSANGUINITY - third degree with atingencia to the second - granted personally by MARIANO SOLER, the first Archbishop of Montevideo. He is written GERENA in his own marriage act while his father is written LORENA in the same sentence: a seventh spelling for this family. HIS BAPTISM IS FOUND: 12 March 1858, San Francisco de Asis, with his sister Ana Rosario. It gives his full name ALFREDO JUSTINIANO and his grandparents Sancho Lerena and Maria Fernandez. AND IT RESOLVES A PUZZLE: the JUSTINIANO looked like an echo of Justiniana Lenguas, who married into the other branch - a tidy connection that would have been wrong. He is named for his GODFATHER, JUSTINIANO LAVALLEJA, on the same line of the same act. This row used to end: "His baptismal age of 1858 sits oddly against the 35 he gave at his 1892 marriage, which would imply 1857; ages at marriage are the weakest date in genealogy." THE ODDNESS WAS THIS ARCHIVE'S, NOT THE RECORD'S - 20 September 2026. He was not born in 1858. The act read here on 14 September says so outright, and the sentence was never lifted off the page: "que nacio el dia DOS de OCTUBRE ... cincuenta y seis" - born 2 OCTOBER 1856, and baptised at seventeen months. AND THAT MAKES THE MARRIAGE AGE EXACT. On 3 February 1892 a man born 2 October 1856 is thirty-five, which is precisely what he told the priest. The record and the age never disagreed; this register had written the baptism date into the birth column and then blamed the discrepancy on the record. Re-read off the image 20 September: film 007713686 image 2847, which also confirms the paternal grandparents "Don Sancho Lerena y Dona Maria Fernandez", the maternal "Don Juan Traibel y Dona Ysabel Centeno", and the godparents Justiniano Lavalleja and Dona Polonia Traibel. HIS WIFE'S SURNAME WAS WRONG HERE FOR A WEEK, AND IS CORRECTED OFF THE IMAGE: SHE IS A GAVAZZO, NOT A GAVARRO, and her mother a SUAREZ, not a Juarez. The 1892 act was held from a machine full-text transcript; the image was opened on 21 September 2026 and reads 'hija legitima de Don GERONIMO GAVAZZO y Dona CAROLINA SUAREZ'. The same image confirms what this row already said - 'Don ALFREDO LERENA, soltero, de TREINTA Y CINCO anos de edad'. AND THE END OF HIS LIFE HAS A CEILING AT LAST. Hermenegilda died at Montevideo on 20 June 1940 'de estado VIUDA', so he died between February 1892 and June 1940, aged somewhere between thirty-five and eighty-three. No death record for him has been found.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Amelia LERENA (m. Eduardo ZALSAMENDI)
Unplaced		Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A LERENA WOMAN found 14 September 2026 in the baptism of her son Luis at San Juan Bautista, Pocitos, Montevideo, 15 August 1903 (born 21 June 1903), "hijo legitimo de Eduardo Del [S]uando y Amelia Lerena". HER PARENTS ARE GIVEN AS "AVELINO y CELIDONIA B. de LERENA" - which is a problem worth stating rather than smoothing over. This archive's Avelino Lerena Fernandez married AMBROSIA TRAIBEL and died in 1890. Either he had a second wife, or Celidonia belongs to a DIFFERENT Avelino - and there are already two others in this register. NOT attached to anyone. SEARCHED AND NOT FOUND, 14 September 2026. The Uruguayan indexes hold several Amelia Lerenas and none is this woman: they belong to TRINIDAD, FLORES - daughters of DOROTEO LERENA and ISIDORA CARDOZO. So the AVELINO-and-CELIDONIA problem stands exactly where it was, and the 1903 Pocitos act remains the only record of her. A NOTE WORTH KEEPING THOUGH: the LERENA-CARDOZO pairing recurs. The 1911 dispensation has two Lerena siblings marrying two Cardozo siblings, and here is a Doroteo Lerena married to an Isidora Cardozo at Trinidad. The Trinidad kindred is already in this register. Two families that married each other more than once. HER HUSBAND'S SURNAME WAS NEVER 'DEL SUANDO'. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026. The act of 15 August 1903 at San Juan Bautista, Pocitos reads: 'bautice solemnemente a MARIA MARGARITA, que nacio el VEINTIUNO DE JUNIO de este ano; hija legitima de EDUARDO ZALSAMENDI y AMELIA LERENA. Abuelos paternos: JUAN ZALSAMENDI y DOLORES POU de ZALSAMENDI. Abuelos maternos: AVELINO [Lerena] y CELIDONIA B. de LERENA. Fueron padrinos ADOLFO LERENA y MARIA ANGELICA YUBIA.' So the machine gave DEL SUANDO and the page gives ZALSAMENDI, a Basque name this archive would never have reached by searching for the other. The initial letter is read as Z and could be S, since SALSAMENDI is the commoner spelling of the same surname and this hand will not settle it. And the act names a Lerena man this register did not hold: the godfather ADOLFO LERENA, standing at her daughter's font - very likely her brother, and entered here as his own row. Ana Rosario LERENA Traibel
Unplaced		1855-01-14 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Daughter of Avelino Lerena and Ambrosia Traibel, baptised at San Francisco de Asis on 12 March 1858 with her brother Alfredo Justiniano. THEY WERE NOT TWINS, AND THIS ROW SAID THEY PROBABLY WERE UNTIL 20 SEPTEMBER 2026. Both acts were re-read off the image that day - film 007713686 image 2847 - and each states its own birth. Hers: "que nacio el dia CATORCE de ENERO ... cincuenta y cinco" - 14 JANUARY 1855. Her brother's: 2 October 1856. They are twenty-one months apart and were carried to the font together, she at three years old and he at seventeen months. "Probably twins" was an inference from a shared baptism date, and the acts refute it. THIS FAMILY BAPTISED IN BATCHES, YEARS LATE, MORE THAN ONCE: Avelino's son Alejandro was christened at La Mauricia in January 1846 among a group of children of various ages, and here are two more, three years and seventeen months old. A granddaughter of Sancho. Her godparents were CONSTANTINO and ANA LAVALLEJA. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713686 image 02847, folio 176, San Francisco de Asis - the two acts of 12 March 1858 that attach the Lerena Traibel branch to Sancho. EVERY NAME ON THEM SURVIVES THE CHECK, word for word. AND HER BIRTH DATE IS NOW READ, NOT TRANSCRIBED: 'que nacio el dia CATORCE DE ENERO de mil ocho cientos cincuenta y cinco, hija legitima de Don Avelino Lerena y de Dona Ambrosia Traibel, NATURALES DE ESTA REPUBLICA.' Andres LERENA Traibel
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A TENTH CHILD OF AVELINO AND AMBROSIA, dated 21 September 2026 from the act itself. FamilySearch indexes him as 'born 16 March 1896, christened 25 November 1896', which is six years after his father died. The act reads: 'En treinta y uno de Diciembre de mil ochocientos sesenta y uno. El Canonigo Dn. JUAN JOSE BRID, bautizo solemnemente a ANDRES, NACIDO EL DIEZ Y SEIS DE MARZO DE MIL OCHOCIENTOS CINCUENTA Y SIETE, hijo legitimo de Dn. Avelino Lerena y de Da. Ambrosia Traibel, ORIENTALES. Padrinos Dn. AVELINO LERENA (HIJO).' Born 16 MARCH 1857 and baptised at four years old, on the last day of 1861 - the same day his brother Juan Arturo was christened at the same parish, aged ten months. HIS GODFATHER IS HIS ELDEST BROTHER, and the parish wrote the disambiguation this archive spent two days arriving at: 'AVELINO LERENA (HIJO)' - Avelino the son, which is Avelino Eduardo, and not his father of the same name. AND HE SHARES A BIRTHDAY WITH A BROTHER: Pedro Alberto Ysabelino was born 16 March 1852, five years to the day before him. That coincidence is what made the index's duplicated '16 March 1896' look like machine noise, and it is not - the index keeps the day and the month and replaces only the year. AND THE YEAR IN THAT ACT CANNOT STAND. THE BIRTH COLUMN IS DELIBERATELY EMPTY, 21 September 2026. The archive's own contradiction gate refused the build the moment this row was written, and it was right: ALFREDO JUSTINIANO WAS BORN 2 OCTOBER 1856 AND ANDRES IS HERE SAID TO BE BORN 16 MARCH 1857 - five and a half months later, to the same mother. Both cannot be true. THE WORDS WERE RE-READ AT DOUBLE MAGNIFICATION BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CONCLUDED and they are not ambiguous: 'mil ochocientos CINCUENTA Y SIETE'. So the fault is not in the reading. THE WEAKER WITNESS IS THIS ONE, AND THE REASON IS THAT IT IS A COPY. Andres's year survives only in an 1896 transcript of an 1861 act - two hands between the font and the page read here. Alfredo's comes from the ORIGINAL register of San Francisco de Asis, and it is corroborated independently: a man born 2 October 1856 is thirty-five on 3 February 1892, which is the age he gave the priest at his own wedding. WHAT IS KEPT: the day and the month, 16 MARCH, and the baptism of 31 December 1861. WHAT IS NOT: the year. He is somewhere between 1857 and 1861 and this archive does not know where. THE TEST, and it is a specific one: the ORIGINAL entry, libro 7o de Bautismos of Nuestra Senora del Carmen del Cordon, pagina 378 - not the 1896 certificate of it.		record ↗

Avelino Eduardo LERENA Traibel
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A FORK THIS ARCHIVE REFUSED TO CHOOSE IS NOW DECIDED, 20 September 2026. On 16 September a working log found a branch indexed LERENA VILLADEMOROS and set out two readings without picking one: either Avelino Lerena Fernandez (b.1815) married twice, Ambrosia Traibel and then Celedonia Villademoros, or Avelino Eduardo is a SON of his. It is the son. Two separate parish acts give this man's own father as AVELINO SERENA/LERENA with AMBROSIA TRAIBEL named on the record - so he is a child of that marriage, and the Villademoros children are Sancho's great-grandchildren. HE IS THEREFORE A NEW CHILD OF AVELINO AND AMBROSIA, beside Alejandro, Josefa, Ana Rosario, Alfredo Justiniano and Mercedes. He married CELEDONIA VILLADEMOROS, daughter of Pedro Villademoros and Angelica Mendez. INDEX-LEVEL: both acts are machine-indexed and neither image has been opened here. AND THE TEST NAMED ABOVE WAS RUN THE SAME EVENING AND CAME BACK EMPTY. LERENA with spouse surname VILLADEMOROS, Uruguay: 6,352 records, and not one of them is a marriage act. Every hit is a parent entry on somebody else's baptism or civil registration. The control is the one that worked two hours earlier: the same query shape on LERENA with spouse JUANICO puts the marriage act of 19 December 1864 at RANK ONE. So the shape finds a marriage when there is one to find. NEITHER VILLADEMOROS MARRIAGE IS IN THE INDEXED RECORD, and the brothers-and-sisters reading stays an inference. It would be settled by the parish marriage books of the Cordon and Union, which have not been walked. AND HE HAS MORE CHILDREN THAN THIS ARCHIVE COULD SEE, FOUND 21 September 2026 BY DELETING A SEARCH PARAMETER. A parent-pair query on him with CELEDONIA VILLADEMOROS returns ZERO. Dropping the mother's SURNAME and keeping only 'Celedonia' returns SEVEN - including the two christenings this archive already held, which is how the zero was known to be false. The seven add: PEDRO SEGUNDO LERENA VILLADEMOROS, who died at Montevideo on 23 August 1936 aged 66, certificate 150, married to PAULINA PRANDO ALGAR; an AVELINO LERENA who died at Montevideo on 5 January 1914 aged 45, certificate 9, married to CARMEN LORENTE, whose mother is written CELEDONIA VILLADEMORO without the s; and marriage registrations of 1921 for both Avelino Pedro Joaquin Silvestre and Pedro Gualberto. WHETHER THAT IS FOUR CHILDREN OR TWO IS NOT SETTLED AND IS NOT GUESSED HERE. Pedro Segundo's estimated 1870 sits on Pedro Gualberto's birth of 19 October 1869; the 1914 Avelino's estimated 1869 sits four years off Avelino Pedro Joaquin Silvestre's December 1865 - and a man who died in 1914 cannot register a marriage in 1921. The ages come from death certificates and are the weakest field there is. TEST: the two 1921 marriage registrations, which would show which of these men were alive to marry.		no public link

Avelino LERENA Fernandez (son of Sancho)
Unplaced		1815 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE HEAD OF THE LERENA TRAIBEL BRANCH, named at last on 14 September 2026. Husband of AMBROSIA TRAIBEL and father of Josefa Lerena Traibel, born 1851. This archive has referred to the Lerena Traibel since its first week without ever placing Avelino. HE IS SANCHO'S SON - SETTLED 14 September 2026 ON A PARISH ACT. An Avelino Lerena stood godfather at the Catedral de Montevideo in 1833 to a child of Justa Pastora Lerena, Sancho's daughter, which is suggestive and no more. No record found here names Avelino's own parents. AND HE IS NAMED IN A PARISH ACT, not only in a death index: the baptisms of his granddaughters at Union on 8 March 1880 give "abuelos maternos AVELINO y AMBROSIA TRAIBEL". A SECOND CHILD FOUND, 14 September 2026: ALFREDO, born about 1857, married at the Catedral Basilica on 3 February 1892 - "hijo legitimo de Don AVELINO LORENA y Dona Ambrosia Traibel". So Avelino and Ambrosia had at least JOSEFA (1851) and ALFREDO (c.1857), and probably MERCEDES, who stood godmother in 1880. TWO BAPTISMS OF 12 MARCH 1858 AT SAN FRANCISCO DE ASIS name the paternal grandparents of his children as SANCHO LERENA and MARIA FERNANDEZ - twice, on one page. So the whole LERENA TRAIBEL line descends from Sancho, and several of its members were already floating unattached in this register: Mercedes Micaela and Clorinda in the dispensation table, and JUAN ARTURO - the man this archive conflated with another Arturo in its first week and had to retract. And the godparents at those two fonts were LAVALLEJA - Justiniano, Constantino and Ana. AND HE NOW HAS DATES, 14 September 2026: BORN 1815, DIED 22 AUGUST 1890 at Montevideo, registered the next day. The registration names his parents Sancho and Maria Fernandez - an INDEPENDENT attestation of the parentage the 1858 baptisms settled, and the first record in this archive to say when Avelino lived. IT ALSO EXPLAINS SOMETHING THE ARCHIVE HAD ONLY NOTED AS ODD. At his son Alfredo's wedding in February 1892 the witness was AMBROSIA TRAIBEL, the mother, where a father would be expected. Avelino had been dead sixteen months. Three records - the 1858 fonts, this registration, and the 1892 marriage - now lock together in one order. The registration spells him LEVENA, an eighth spelling. The image has not been opened, so the dates are SECONDARY; the parentage is PRIMARY from 1858. AND AN ELDEST CHILD, found 14 September 2026 - by correcting one of this archive's own rows. ALEJANDRO, born 29 May 1843, baptised at La Mauricia on 7 January 1846, "hijo legitimo de Avelino Serena y Ambrosia Traibel" - a boy this archive had wrongly recorded as a sixth child of his brother Luis. So Avelino and Ambrosia were married before May 1843, and their known children run ALEJANDRO (1843), JOSEFA (1851), ALFREDO JUSTINIANO and ANA ROSARIO (1858), probably MERCEDES. And the act shows the two brothers standing as godparents for each other's children at the same font on the same morning - Luis and Justiniana for Alejandro, while Luis's own two were christened in the entries just above. AND HE DID NOT MARRY TWICE - DECIDED 20 September 2026. The LERENA VILLADEMOROS branch found on 16 September was left as two readings: a second marriage of his, or a son of his. It is a son. Two parish acts give AVELINO EDUARDO LERENA's own father as Avelino with Ambrosia Traibel on the record, so Avelino Eduardo joins Alejandro, Josefa, Ana Rosario, Alfredo Justiniano and Mercedes as a child of this marriage, and Celedonia Villademoros is a daughter-in-law. NINE CHILDREN, 21 September 2026, where this row listed six. A parent-pair search on Avelino with Ambrosia added four: PEDRO ALBERTO YSABELINO (b. 16 March 1852, and he is the ALBERTO who died in 1921), JUAN ARTURO (b. 16 February 1861), MERCEDES MICAELA (b. 8 May 1863) - who is very likely the Mercedes who stood godmother in 1880 - and MARIA ADELINA, who married Carlos Alberto Fein and died in 1946. With Alejandro, Josefa, Ana Rosario, Alfredo Justiniano and Avelino Eduardo that is nine, and the births run from 1843 to about 1865 - twenty-two years, which is one marriage and not two. A THIRD INDEPENDENT ACT NAMES HIS PARENTS: the 1852 Aguada baptism gives the paternal grandparents as Sancho Lerena and Maria Fernandez, after the two of 1858 and the christening of 1866. AND THE 1852 ACTS CALL HIM AN ORIENTAL: 'orientales los padres' at the Aguada, which agrees with a birth at Montevideo in 1815. The same page then calls SANCHO oriental too, and that one this archive does not accept - see the working log. TEN, WITHIN THE HOUR. ANDRES, born 16 March 1857, was read off the second opening of the same certificate book. And his sons appear as godfathers to his daughters: Avelino Eduardo stood for Andres in 1861, written 'AVELINO LERENA (HIJO)', and an AMBROSIO LERENA - almost certainly the Carlos Ambrosio of the family chart - stood for Maria Adelina in 1865. The births now run 1843, 1851, 1852, 1855, 1856, 1857, 1861, 1863, 1865 with Avelino Eduardo earlier still, which is a single marriage producing a child about every two years for twenty-two years. SEVEN OF HIS CHILDREN LODGED BAPTISMAL CERTIFICATES TOGETHER IN NOVEMBER 1896, and this archive thinks it knows why. Certificates 147 to 153 of one Montevideo book, consecutive, drawn from FOUR different parishes - the Aguada, San Agustin de la Union, San Francisco de Asis and the Cordon - and registered within days of each other: Mercedes Micaela, Josefina, Pedro Alberto Ysabelino, Ana Rosario, Juan Arturo, Andres and Maria Adelina. Grown brothers and sisters do not each happen to want proof of baptism in the same week. That is what proving FILIATION looks like, and their father had died on 22 August 1890. Offered as a hypothesis with a named test: the Montevideo succession and notarial records of 1896. See data/1852-aguada-union-certificates.tsv. AND THE PARISH WAS WRONG, CORRECTED 21 September 2026 OFF THE PLATE. This archive placed these 1880 baptisms at the UNION, which is what the index's place field says. The act itself reads 'con licencia del infrascripto Cura Parroco DE ESTA BASILICA MENOR Y CATEDRAL DE MONTEVIDEO, bautice solemnemente EN ELLA a Maria Carlota'. They were baptised in the CATEDRAL BASILICA, not at the Union.		GRO index — no public per-record link

THE APEX OF THE FIRST SCHEMA, and a person this archive never held until the plate was opened. The diagram puts him alone at the top, with lines down to BERNABE LERENA and MARIA LERENA - so he is the father of both, and the grandfather of the groom Custodio Lerena. HE EXISTS BECAUSE A PRIEST DREW HIM. No act read here names him otherwise, he has no dates, and the diagram gives him no wife; a kinship schema draws only the line it has to prove. Carlos Augusto Federico LERENA Salvanach (b. 9 July 1883)
Unplaced · high interest		1883-07-09 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Retiro, Buenos Aires
IMAGE READ 14 September 2026, and two things this row got wrong are now fixed. It called the record a "Retiro BAPTISM"; it is an INFORMACION MATRIMONIAL, the sworn pre-marriage enquiry. And it read the father as ALBERTO, guessing GILBERTO - the act says GILBERTO plainly, so the guess can stop being a guess. "Don Carlos Lerena, natural de la Rep. Oriental, bautizado en la Iglesia de el CORDON, Dep. de Montevideo, de treinta anos, soltero, domiciliado en Pueyrredon 1750, hijo de D. Gilberto Lerena y de Dona Julia Salvanach." He married ROSA MARIA EPPENS. This IDENTIFIES the CEMLA couple 'Carlos Lerena 41 Uruguayan' and 'Rosa M. E. Lerena 39 Argentine' arriving Buenos Aires from Montevideo 27 May 1926 - two independent sources, same couple. AND IT NAMES WHERE HIS BAPTISM IS: the Iglesia del CORDON, Montevideo - a third Montevideo parish for this family, after San Francisco de Asis and the Catedral Basilica. THREE REGISTER ROWS WERE THIS ONE MAN, MERGED 20 September 2026. This archive had him as "Carlos Augusto Federico LERENA" (born 9 July 1883, from Uruguayan civil registration), as "Carlos LERENA Salvanach" (the 1914 informacion matrimonial), and as "Carlos LERENA (b.1885, arr. 1926)" (the CEMLA crossing). The note above had already identified the second with the third - "two independent sources, same couple" - and the rows were never joined. The arithmetic settles the first: the act of 25 March 1914 calls him "de treinta anos", and a man born 9 July 1883 is exactly thirty that March. Same parents, same birthplace, same city, and the CEMLA wife "Rosa M. E. Lerena" is ROSA MARIA EPPENS, whom the act names. And the merge exposed a file disagreeing with the register. The 1914 act calls him SOLTERO at thirty, while data/relations.tsv gave him a wife ADELA CAMIGLIA and a son AVELINO CARLOS baptised at Mendoza in 1904. That contradiction was already settled here and the relations file had not been told: the row for ROSA MARIA EPPENS has said since 14 September that her Carlos is NOT Adela Camiglia's - "two men, one name, and the register now says so on both rows". It said so on the register rows and not on the edges. The Mendoza man now has a row of his own and the edges point at him. Custodio LERENA
Unplaced		Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE GROOM of the eleventh Lerena dispensation, 1911. Son of Bernabe Lerena and Florinda Cardozo. Petitioned to marry his DOUBLE FIRST COUSIN Ema Cardozo - the tie runs through both his father's and his mother's side, which is why the petition needed a diagram. They asked to be excused the fee, "pues no pueden satisfacerlos" - a poor Lerena household, in an archive that has spent two days finding generals, judges and hacendados. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713362 image 02473 - and the folio is not prose but 'Esquema explicativo de los grados de parentesco', TWO KINSHIP DIAGRAMS the priest drew himself to prove the impediment. AND THE DIAGRAM EXPLAINS HIS NAME. The apex of the second schema is CUSTODIO CARDOZO, his mother's father - so the groom of 1911 is named for the grandfather whose two children's marriages created the impediment he had to petition against.
