Don Luis Eugenio LERENA LENGUAS
Unplaced · high interest		1845-02-09 · Estado Oriental del Uruguay · Uruguayan · HACENDADO (landowner) · found in Rosario, Santa Fe
DOCUMENTED, 13 September 2026. "Natural de esta Republica", married Dona Julia Juanico at San Francisco de Asis, Ciudad Vieja, Montevideo, on 19 December 1864 - act 258, image read - which names him "hijo legitimo de Don Luis y de Dona Justiniana Lenguas". His daughter Laura Adela's baptism of 1869 names the same two as her paternal grandparents, independently. So his parentage is PRIMARY, not the tree assertion it was that morning; and it makes him a brother of GILBERTO JUSTINIANO LERENA LENGUAS, co-founder of the Argentine Stud Book - though THAT brotherhood still rests on published genealogy rather than an act, and is marked secondary on the chart. Five children, all on primary records. Died before 11 July 1910, when his widow's death was registered. NO LINK TO JUAN CARLOS LERENA HAS BEEN SHOWN. HIS DEATH IS FOUND: 24 DECEMBER 1901, at seven in the morning, at the house calle Suarez No. 25 in el Paso del Molino, Montevideo. Act 216. "De nacionalidad oriental, nacido en Montevideo, de cincuenta y seis [anos], de estado casado, y de profesion HACENDADO" - a landowner, which is the first statement of this family's standing anywhere in the archive. He died of pulmonary tuberculosis, certified by Dr Scremini. IT WAS HIDING UNDER A MISSPELLING: FamilySearch indexes him as Luis SERENA Lenguas, so every search under LERENA missed him, including a 1,502-row sweep of Montevideo Lerena deaths run the same morning. He was found by searching for his MOTHER. The act also says his father Luis Lerena was already dead, and that his mother Justiniana Lenguas was ALIVE AT 79 and staying in the same house. HIS OWN BAPTISM IS NOW READ: born 9 FEBRUARY 1843, baptised 7 January 1846 at the Capilla de la Mauricia, Villa de la Union. "Hijo legitimo de Luis Lerena y Justiniana Lenguas, ORIENTALES. Paternos: Francisco y Maria Fernandez. Maternos: Pedro y Carmen Gonzalez." Godparents Juan Lenguas and Maria Fernandez. His death act of 1901 gave his age as 56, implying 1845; the act of his own baptism is the better evidence. A THIRD INDEPENDENT RECORD OF THAT DEATH, 14 September 2026: the Montevideo cemetery office's own return, "Relacion de los individuos que han fallecido en el mes de Diciembre de 1901", lists him against day 24 between Lucrecia Sosa and Emilia Zanola de Sanchez. So he was BURIED AT MONTEVIDEO, and the date now rests on three documents - the civil act, the cemetery roll, and his widow's sworn statement of 1908. Found only by searching the COMPOUND surname. A CONTRADICTION INSIDE THE REGISTER ITSELF, recorded 14 September 2026 and NOT resolved. Luis Eugenio's act says he was born 9 FEBRUARY 1843; his sister Justiniana Mauricia Maxima's act, the entry immediately before his on the same page in the same hand, says she was born 30 JULY 1843. That is FIVE AND A HALF MONTHS between two full siblings, and both cannot be right. Neither is a machine reading: the transcript of each act has been read verbatim, and both say "del pasado ano cuarenta y tres". THE ERROR IS IN THE SOURCE, NOT IN THIS ARCHIVE. Every act on that page is a LATER TRANSCRIPT copied out of a legajo by order of Bishop Jacinto Vera - a clerk working from loose papers three years after the events, who had already written "ano cuarenta y tres" twice on the same page. A carried-forward year is the likeliest explanation and it is a guess. Luis Eugenio's death registration of 1901 gives an age and has been read; testing it against 1843 is the next step. AND THE CONTRADICTION IS RESOLVED THE SAME AFTERNOON - AGAINST HIS OWN BAPTISM ACT. Three facts, two of them already in this archive: (1) his baptism act says he was born 9 February of "el pasado ano cuarenta y tres"; (2) his sister Justiniana Mauricia's act, THE ENTRY IMMEDIATELY ABOVE HIS IN THE SAME HAND, says she was born 30 July 1843 - five and a half months before him, which is impossible; (3) his civil death registration of 24 December 1901 gives his age as FIFTY-SIX, which puts his birth at about 1845. Two independent constraints point to 1845 and only one points to 1843 - and the one is a clerk copying loose papers three years after the fact, who wrote "ano cuarenta y tres" in the entry above this one and again in the entry below. A YEAR CARRIED FORWARD DOWN A COLUMN IS THE ORDINARY WAY THIS ERROR HAPPENS, and it is the only reading under which his sister's date, his own death record and his baptism can all stand. SO THIS ROW NOW CARRIES 9 FEBRUARY 1845. The day and month are secure and are not in question; only the year moved. The act's own words are kept above and in data/1846-la-mauricia-page.tsv, because the source says 1843 and a reader is entitled to see that this archive overruled it and why. THIS IS AN INFERENCE, NOT A DOCUMENT - an age at death is the weakest date in genealogy and this archive has said so in print; it is load-bearing here only because it agrees with a constraint reached independently of it.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Don Luis LERENA (the godfather) = probably Luis LERENA y Fernandez
Unplaced		1811-02-09 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Rosario, Santa Fe
Stood godfather at Rosario in 1866 but did not attend - represented by Don Claudino Campos, which is what one does from the other side of the river. THAT HE IS THE CHILD'S GRANDFATHER IS NOW DOCUMENTED, NOT INFERRED: the Montevideo marriage act of 19 December 1864 names the groom "hijo legitimo de Don Luis y de Dona Justiniana Lenguas", and Laura Adela's baptism of 1869 names "abuelos paternos Don Luis y Dona Justiniana Lenguas" - two acts, independently. What remains SECONDARY is only the equation with LUIS LERENA y FERNANDEZ (1811-1878) of the published trees, and through him the line up to Sancho. The 1866 child's fifth given name is JUSTINIANA, for that grandmother. AND HIS OWN PARENTS ARE NOW IN AN ACT: "Paternos: D. Sancho y Dona Maria Fernandez", from his son Alberto Octavio's baptism of 7 October 1850. The "y Fernandez" of the published trees is his mother's name, and it is correct. Two 1846 transcripts give the father as FRANCISCO instead; they are copies, and the 1850 original is preferred. HIS MARRIAGE ACT IS FOUND AND READ, 14 September 2026 - and it says two things nobody here knew. He married Justiniana Lenguas on 3 MARCH 1842, aged THIRTY and "natural de la Ciudad de Montevideo", so born about 1812. His parents are given as "D. SANCHO y Dona MARIA FERNANDEZ" - the third act to say Sancho, and the earliest. AND HE WAS A WIDOWER: "desposé en SEGUNDAS NUPCIAS ... viudo de Dona CAROLINA". He had a first wife who was dead by March 1842, and this archive has never looked for her or for any children of that marriage. HIS FIRST MARRIAGE IS FOUND TOO: to DONA CAROLINA OLIVA, by petition at Montevideo on 3 SEPTEMBER 1836, signed in his own hand - the only signature of this man anywhere in the archive. He asked to be excused the usual enquiry "en atencion a ser ambos nativos de esta Ciudad, y bastante conocidos". HIS BIRTH IS NOW EXACT: 9 FEBRUARY 1811, and his own baptism act is in this archive for the first time. Iglesia Matriz, Montevideo, 10 February 1811: "bautice solemnemente a LUIS ESCOLASTICO que nacio ayer, hijo legitimo de Don Sancho Llerena natural de Andalucia y de Dona Maria Fernandez natural de Asturias". Padrinos Don Luis Jose Avila y Dona Juana Josefa Guispez; officiating Presbitero Juan Loberas. This row carried c.1812 from an inference; the trees said 1811 and nothing more. It also puts his SECOND forename on record - ESCOLASTICO - which nothing here had.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Ema Sixta LERENA Juanico
Unplaced		1876-11-21 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Married JOSE PEDRO MASSERA (1866-1942), professor of philosophy at the Universidad de la Republica and later a Colorado senator; their son was the mathematician Jose Luis Massera. The witnesses at her wedding were her sister Maria and her brother-in-law Jaime Herrera - the couple of the 1886 act. Aged thirty-three on the act, so born about 1880; a findmypast index says 1876. She is the "Emma Liseta, 1876, father Luis, mother Julia" copied from a findmypast index on 9 September, four days before the family was identified. Named SIXTA for Sixta Lenguas Gonzalez, her mother's stepmother. HER BIRTH DATE IS NOW SETTLED AT 21 NOVEMBER 1876, not c.1880. Three independent records agree: the civil birth registration of 30 May 1903, the Uruguay Baptisms index, and her christening at the Catedral on 8 January 1881. The c.1880 came from her stated age at marriage in 1913, and ages at marriage are the weakest date in genealogy. HER MARRIAGE DISPENSATION IS FOUND, 14 September 2026, and it is a NINTH Lerena dispensation this archive's own sweep of that register had missed. On 19 June 1913 Jose Pedro Massera petitioned the Provisor to be dispensed "una conciliar proclama" in order to marry "Dona EMA SIXTA LERENA JUANICO, FELIGRESA DE LA METROPOLITANA", the date of the wedding being already fixed - it took place eleven days later. The sweep missed her because its formula was "LERENA FELIGRESA" and her COMPOUND SURNAME sits between those two words.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Felix LERENA (father of Sancho)
Connected		Spain, presumably Andalusia (his son is "natural de Andalucia"; CORDOBA comes from a tree) · Spanish · found in Spain
NAMED ON AN ACT, 15 September 2026 - the first time this archive has had a generation above Sancho on anything but a tree. The baptism of their grandson LUIS ESCOLASTICO at the Iglesia Matriz of Montevideo, 10 February 1811, gives "Abuelos paternos FELIZ GERENA y ROSALIA AGUILAR" in the same sentence that calls their son "Don SANCHO LLERENA natural de ANDALUCIA" . GERENA is the machine's G-for-L - a seventh spelling for this family, and the same mangling that produced the still-unresolved "Dona Andugara GERENA" in the Santa Rosa de Lima confirmations at Rosario. He is written FELIZ, the ordinary period spelling of FELIX. WHAT IS STILL NOT KNOWN ABOUT THEM: everything else. No dates, no places, no marriage, no other children. The Geneanet pages carry nothing but the line "Family: Revista 15 IEGU", and one Spanish tree offers Cordoba about 1725 and a marriage about 1753 - all values marked "about", with no source at all, and giving Sancho a birth of ca 1754 that the 1812 padron contradicts. So the PARENTAGE is documented and the PEOPLE are not. The search that follows from this is FELIX LERENA and ROSALIA AGUILAR in CORDOBA and it has not been run against Spanish parish records. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT since 14 September. Film 007713375 image 02410, Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, the baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 February 1811 - the only record in this archive that names Sancho's own parents. AND THE PLATE CORRECTS HIS SURNAME. The machine transcript read the line as 'Abuelos paternos FELIZ GERENA y ROS[A]LIA AGUILAR', and this archive inferred FELIX LERENA from it. The page itself reads 'Ab.s pat.s FELIX LLERENA y Ros[alia Aguilar]' - FELIX, with an X, and LLERENA WITH THE DOUBLE L, in the same hand that writes his son 'Sancho Llerena' two lines above. So the earliest Spanish generation this archive can name spelled it LLERENA.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Fidel LERENA (m. Maria Ruedas)
Unplaced · high interest		found in Montevideo
A FIDEL LERENA WITH A WIFE AND A GRANDSON, named on a Montevideo death registration of 1889. "Abuelos paternos FIDEL LERENA y MARIA RUEDAS" - the paternal grandparents of Victor Lerena, who died that year at twenty-eight. FIDEL is a rare forename in this family and appears exactly once elsewhere: Sancho Llerena's son, born about 1799. And this archive worked out only yesterday where that name came from - Don Fidel Rey of Montevideo, godfather at Agustina's baptism in 1795. WHETHER THIS FIDEL IS SANCHO'S SON IS NOT ESTABLISHED. Sancho's Fidel was born about 1799 and the Geneanet trees give him a death in 1822, at twenty-two or twenty-three. For this to be the same man, Pedro would have to be born by 1822 and fathering Victor at about forty. That is tight but possible, and the tree's 1822 is tree-only and has never been tested against a Montevideo burial. The alternative is a second Fidel Lerena at Montevideo, which nothing rules out. Recorded as a branch to test, not as a descent. This row said "THE TEST IS CHEAP AND HAS NOT BEEN RUN: a Montevideo marriage of FIDEL LERENA to MARIA RUEDAS, and a burial for Fidel around 1822." IT WAS RUN ON 20 SEPTEMBER 2026 AND BOTH HALVES CAME BACK EMPTY. No marriage of a Fidel Lerena to a Maria Rueda or Ruedas is indexed for Uruguay - control: the same query shape on Luis and Juanico returns the marriage act of 19 December 1864 as its first result. And no burial of a Fidel Lerena between 1815 and 1840 - control: Rodriguez in that window returns 62,026 records with real burials, so the shelf is not empty. THE SURPRISE IS WHICH WAY IT CUTS. The tree death of 1822 was the one thing that would have kept this Fidel apart from Sancho's son, and it is not in the record either. The two are still NOT merged - an unevidenced death is not evidence of survival - but nothing now separates them except the silence. See data/fs-session-2026-09-20.tsv. THE GRANDSON'S DATES ARE NOW EXACT, 20 September 2026: Victor Lerena, born 1861 at Montevideo, died 10 October 1889 - so the "twenty-eight" this row has carried is right to the year. His father is indexed PEDRO LORENA, which is the spelling the register already holds for him. The test that would settle Fidel himself was attempted on 20 September and could not be run - the API reached for it does not filter on a spouse surname. See data/fs-session-2026-09-20.tsv. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 008165116 image 01442, folio 94 - Montevideo civil death registration No. 622 of 1889. 'Abuelos [paternos] FIDEL LERENA y MARIA RUEDAS' - on the plate, in the same hand that writes Pedro Lerena on the line above. Whether this Fidel is Sancho's son remains unestablished, and nothing on this plate bears on it.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Montevideo civil death registration 1889 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ, ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS2W-27FX-P" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-CS2W-27FX-P</a> - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 008165116 image 01442
Fidel LERENA Fernandez
Connected		1799 · Canelones ("el Canelon") · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo, calle San Juan No 60
ONE OF SANCHO'S SIX CHILDREN LISTED IN THE 1812 PADRON, and the first time any of them has been entered here. The household at calle San Juan No 60 held Sancho "andaluz" 45, Maria Fernandez 36, and six children all "naturales del CANELON" - Juan Agustin 21, Pedro Antonio 19, Agustina 17, Fidel 12, Justa 10 and Dominga 7 - together with Sancho's father-in-law RODRIGO FERNANDEZ, 63, asturiano, and four enslaved people. That all six were born at Canelones is the best support the 1791 marriage venue has, and it comes from a census rather than from a tree. NOT AN ACT: this row rests on a published transcription of a census, reported at second hand through a family tree. The act has not been opened here. Aged 12 in the 1812 padron, so born about 1800; the tree gives 1799 and a death in 1822, at twenty-two or twenty-three. If that death is right he is the only one of Sancho's children this archive can see dying young, and he died in the decade the family were serving the Portuguese occupation at Canelones. The death is tree-only and has not been tested against Montevideo burials. The tree's own prose says the couple had EIGHT children while listing seven, and it omits Dominga, whom the padron and a Canelones act of 1804 both attest - so its child list is incomplete and this row inherits that caution. AND HIS NAME IS EXPLAINED, 15 September 2026. It appears nowhere else in this family and this row could not account for it. His sister Agustina's baptism of 30 August 1795 gives her godfather as DON FIDEL REY, vecino de Montevideo. The child born four years later carries the godfather's name. An inference, not a statement - no act says the boy was named for him - but the coincidence of a rare forename and a recent sponsor in one household is a thin one to explain away. AND THAT DEATH HAS NOW BEEN TESTED AGAINST MONTEVIDEO BURIALS, 20 September 2026: IT IS NOT THERE. A search of Uruguayan deaths 1815-1840 for a Fidel Lerena returns nothing, against a control in the same window - Rodriguez - returning 62,026 records with burials of 1840 and 1844 on the first screen. The 1822 remains tree-only, and is now tree-only having been looked for. It can no longer be used to rule this man out of the 1889 death registration that names a FIDEL LERENA and MARIA RUEDAS as a grandfather and grandmother.		no public link

Gilberto LERENA (b.1879, arr. 1928)
Unplaced · high interest		1879 · ARGENTINE · comercio · found in arr. 1928-02-27
An ARGENTINE-national Lerena born within three years of Pablo Armando, carrying the Gilberto name of the Stud Book family. Travelling with a son of the same name. CORRECTED 20 September 2026. This row used to end "Not in the 1895 census household of Gilberto (1854)", and that was the only thing holding it apart from another row in this register. It is wrong. Line 6 of that schedule reads Salvanach Lerena, GILBERTO, varon, 17, soltero, R. Oriental - Gilberto's eldest son, seventeen in 1895, so born 1877 or 1878, who is already here as Gilberto LERENA Salvanach (the younger, b.1878). WHAT FITS, NOW THAT THE OBJECTION IS GONE: a birth year one out; Argentine nationality, which the Salvanach son demonstrably holds (Montevideo-born, yet in Argentine military records 1911-1936); Buenos Aires; and a marriage of 19 May 1906, against which a son born 1916 sits naturally - and that son is the estudiante of twelve travelling beside him, whom this row had noted as a curiosity without explaining. DELIBERATELY NOT MERGED. Both rows are index-level, and this archive once conflated two Arturo Lerenas on a forename and an age and retracted the branch within the hour. THE TEST, AND IT IS CHEAP: the Argentine military record carries a date of birth and the CEMLA entry carries marital status. Either one settles it. AND THE SON IS NO LONGER A CURIOSITY, 20 September 2026. Gilberto Lerena Salvanach married CIPRIANA SAENZ PENA on 19 May 1906 and a Capital Federal index names their child "Gilberto de Jesus del Corazon". The son this row noted without explaining is a son that man demonstrably had. IT STILL DOES NOT CLOSE. That child carries no indexed birth year - a search of Argentine Lerena births 1910-1920 returns ten records and none is he - so the twelve-year-old of 1928 remains unmatched by a document. Gilberto LERENA (Gilberto Justiniano LERENA LENGUAS)
Unplaced · high interest		1854 · Uruguay · Uruguayan/Argentine · CORREDOR DE COMERCIO (commercial broker), per the 1895 census; breeder and co-founder of the Argentine Stud Book · found in Buenos Aires
Co-founder of the Argentine Stud Book; a Group 1 at Palermo renamed for him in 1914. A Lerena at the centre of Argentine thoroughbred breeding, in the right country at the right time - and PABLO ARMANDO TRAINED AND OWNED RACEHORSES. Born Montevideo 24 May 1855, died 8 December 1912. His father is now documented rather than asserted: his brother Luis Eugenio's marriage act of 19 December 1864 names their parents as Don Luis and Dona Justiniana Lenguas. That he and Luis Eugenio are BROTHERS still rests on published genealogy, not on an act, and is marked secondary on the charts. NO PROVEN LINK TO PABLO ARMANDO. HIS PARENTS ARE NOW NAMED, 13 September 2026: LUIS LERENA and JUSTINIANA LENGUAS. Two independent Uruguayan parish records, entered for two different children of his, both give his parents so. That makes him A BROTHER OF LUIS EUGENIO LERENA LENGUAS - the same Montevideo household that produced the one Lerena baptism in Rosario before 1879. The racing dynasty and the Rosario thread are the same family. THE IMAGE IS NOW OPENED, AND IT IS PRIMARY. Act 1447 of 31 October 1886 baptises his daughter Maria Angelica Julia, "hija legitima de Don Gilberto Lerena y de Dona Julia Salvanach, orientales", and names the paternal grandparents "Don LUIS y Dona JUSTINIANA LENGUAS" - the same couple named as Luis Eugenio's parents in his marriage act of 1864 and his death act of 1901. AND THE GODMOTHER IS DONA JULIA JUANICO DE LERENA - Luis Eugenio's own wife, standing at the font for her husband's brother's daughter. The two households are one family acting as one. Written by Martin Perez, the same priest who wrote act 1720 for Candido Jaime that year. AND A SECOND ACT, FROM A DIFFERENT PARISH, SAYS THE SAME, 14 September 2026: the baptism of his son Raul Alfredo Luis at the Catedral Basilica on 10 September 1885 gives "abuelos paternos: Luis y Justiniana Lenguas" and "abuelos maternos: Cristobal y Angela Alvarez" - identical to act 1447, in a different year, a different parish and a different priest's hand. Its godmother is a JOSEFA LERENA otherwise unknown to this archive. HIS HOUSEHOLD IS NOW ASSEMBLED, 14 September 2026: EIGHT CHILDREN by Julia Salvanach - Gilberto (1878), Carlos (1884), Raul Alfredo Luis (1885), Maria Angelica Julia (1886), all born at Montevideo; then Maria Carlota (1890), Luis Felipe (1892), Cesar Alberto (1896) and Horacio Alfredo (1898), at Buenos Aires. THE LAST MONTEVIDEO BAPTISM IS 31 OCTOBER 1886 AND THE FIRST BUENOS AIRES ONE 29 OCTOBER 1890, which brackets the family's crossing between those dates - firmer than the "1887 to 1889" this archive read off census birthplaces. AND JUAN CARLOS IS NOT AMONG THEM. AND THE 1895 CENSUS SCHEDULE, READ AS A HOUSEHOLD FOR THE FIRST TIME ON 14 September 2026, GIVES HIM A TRADE: CORREDOR DE COMERCIO - a commercial broker, aged 41, casado, R. Oriental, catolico. This archive has had him as a breeder, which is what he is remembered for; in his own house he told the enumerator he was a broker.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Hector Narciso LERENA Bassani
Unplaced		1886-10-29 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · dibujante (draughtsman), 1911 · found in Montevideo, later Buenos Aires
Born 29 October 1886 and registered at Montevideo on 6 November, act N 424, page 14. Son of Alejandro M. Lerena and Julia Bassani, "hijo de los declarantes". HE IS THE EVIDENCE THAT THIS FAMILY WAS IN MONTEVIDEO BEFORE IT WAS IN BUENOS AIRES - the Argentine records for these parents begin with a baptism in 1905, nineteen years later. Appears again in Argentine military records 1911-1936 and in Capital Federal parish records, both attached in the FamilySearch tree to the same man, L8GL-F8B. MERGED 22 SEPTEMBER 2026 WITH THE ROW THAT STOOD HERE AS "Hector LERENA" - an Argentine of Buenos Aires who married AMALIA PACHECO and had a daughter Emilia Cecilia on 20 December 1915. They are one man, and the merge is made on the ACT and not on the tree's attachment. The San Carlos marriage of 25 February 1911 was opened: "hijo legitimo de D. ALEJANDRO MAXIMO [Lerena] ... y de dona JULIA BA[SSANI], natural de Italia ... con dona AMALIA PA[CHECO]". THREE THINGS AGREE AT ONCE - the wife the old row carried, the parents this row carries, and the age. He is "de la Rep. Oriental, de veinte y cuatro" on 25 February 1911, which puts his birth between 26 February 1886 and 25 February 1887 and fits 29 October 1886 exactly. AND IT CORRECTS THE INDEX, WHICH HAS HIM BORN 1887 AT ALMAGRO, BUENOS AIRES. The act says ORIENTAL. The indexer defaulted the birthplace to the event place, which is a thing this collection's index does. His trade on the act is dibujante, draughtsman, and he lived at calle Gurruchaga 1388. HE IS ALSO "hijo LEGITIMO" IN 1911, THOUGH HIS MONTEVIDEO BIRTH ACT OF 1886 RECORDS NO MARRIAGE - his father was a widower and his mother soltera. Either Alejandro and Julia married between 1888 and 1911, or the parish wrote the ordinary word. This archive does not know which, and the marriage of those two has not been found.		record ↗

Jose Julio LERENA Juanico
Unplaced		1879-03-15 · Montevideo, calle Yi 303 · Uruguayan · JUEZ DE PAZ y Oficial del Estado Civil, 1a Seccion de Montevideo (strong, not proved) · found in Montevideo
A SIXTH CHILD of Luis Eugenio Lerena Lenguas and Julia Juanico, found 13 September 2026. Born 15 March 1879 at Montevideo and not registered until 14 September 1908, when he was 29 - a late registration of the kind a man makes to produce proof of birth for his own wedding, and he does appear on a Montevideo marriage index card for 1908. THE IMAGE IS OPENED. Born at the house calle YI No. 303, and registered in 1908 by HIS OWN MOTHER - Dona Julia Juanico, 64, a widow of calle Buenos Aires 102. The act independently confirms Luis Eugenio's death, "fallecido en esta Ciudad el veinte y cuatro de Diciembre de mil novecientos uno", from a different document written seven years after it. AND IT PLACES JUSTINIANA LENGUAS, AT EIGHTY-SEVEN, RESIDENT IN THE ARGENTINE REPUBLIC. AND HE WAS ALMOST CERTAINLY A JUDGE. Thirty-five Montevideo civil marriage acts of 1902-1914 open "Por ante mi JULIO LERENA JUANICO, JUEZ DE PAZ y OFICIAL DEL ESTADO CIVIL de la Primera Seccion del Departamento de Montevideo". The compound surname is this family's and no other here; he is the only JULIO among the seven children; and he would be thirty in 1909. STRONG BUT NOT PROVED - no act read so far gives the judge's own parents. And it explains the oddest thing in his record: a man appointed Oficial del Estado Civil needs proof of his own birth rather more than a bridegroom does, which is why his widowed mother swore it before a Juzgado de Paz in September 1908. Jose Loreto LERENA Fernandez
Connected		1806-12-10 · Canelones · Uruguayan · found in Canelones
AN EIGHTH CHILD OF SANCHO AND MARIA FERNANDEZ, FOUND 15 September 2026, and he is in no tree and no census. Canelones, 11 December 1806: "bautizo solemnemente a un parvulo de un dia de edad y puso por nombre JOSE LORETO, hijo legitimo de Sancho Lorena y de Maria Fernandez vecinos de esta Villa, fue padrino Jose Martinez de la Vega vecino de Montevideo." He was found only because the surname was searched as LORENA. Under LLERENA and LERENA he does not appear. HE IS NOT IN THE 1812 PADRON, which lists six children and no boy of about five. The likeliest reading is that he died before 1812, which is what a household return's silence usually means about a small child - but no burial has been looked for and none is claimed here. The Geneanet tree's own prose says the couple had EIGHT children while naming seven. He may be the eighth. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, and the transcript had one word wrong. Film 008166571 image 01176. The act reads: 'bautizo solemnemente a un parvulo de un dia de edad y puso por nombre JOSE LORETO, hijo legitimo de SANCHO LERENA y de MARIA FERNANDEZ, VECINOS DE ESTA VILLA. Fue padrino JOSE MARTINEZ DE LA VEGA, vecino de MONTEVIDEO, a quien advirtio la obligacion y parentesco, y yo el Cura y Vicario propio de la expresada Parroquia lo firmo y certifico para que conste.' THIS ROW QUOTED THE TRANSCRIPT AS 'SANCHO LORENA'. THE PLATE SAYS LERENA. That is the fourth assertion about this family's spelling to turn out to be the machine's rather than the scribe's in one day. AND 'VECINOS DE ESTA VILLA' IS WORTH MORE THAN THE SPELLING. In December 1806 Sancho and Maria were RESIDENTS OF CANELONES, not visitors to it - which joins up with Agustina's baptism there in 1795, Dominga's in 1804 and Justa Pastora being called 'natural de Canelones' in 1831. The family lived at Canelones for at least eleven years and had moved to Montevideo, calle San Juan No 60, by the padron of 1812. The godfather was brought from Montevideo, which is the other direction.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Josefa LERENA (godmother, 1885)
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A LERENA WOMAN THIS ARCHIVE HAS NEVER MET, found 14 September 2026. She stood godmother to Gilberto Lerena's son Raul at the Catedral Basilica de Montevideo on 10 September 1885, with Alfredo Costa. A woman of the surname at the font of a Lerena child is very likely one of the siblings of that generation - Gilberto had at least five - or a first cousin. Nothing else in this archive names her, and she is worth chasing. NOT A CORRECTION - A CROSS-REFERENCE THIS ROW LACKED, written 20 September 2026. "Nothing else in this archive names her" is still true: no second record names this godmother. But two rows here hold candidates for her and this one sent the reader off to chase somebody already being chased. JOSEFA LERENA TRAIBEL (1851-1947), daughter of Avelino, whose row has called itself a strong candidate for this godmother since 14 September; and "Lerena de L[erena], JOSEFA, 43, viuda", line 4 of Gilberto's 1895 Buenos Aires household, which the household file calls "almost certainly" this woman. AND THE TWO CANDIDATES FIGHT EACH OTHER, which no row here has said. The 1895 enumerator writes wives as [maiden surname] "de L." - Lenguas de Lerena, Salvanach de Lerena - so line 4 is a woman born a Lerena who married a Lerena. But Josefa Lerena Traibel married a MORATORIO, on a parish act, and would have been written "Lerena de Moratorio". So either the "de L." reading is wrong, or the 1895 widow and the Traibel daughter are two different women - and at most one of the two can be this godmother. Three rows, one name, and no act joining any two of them.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Josefa LERENA TRAIBEL (1851-1947)
Unplaced		1851-02-10 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
DAUGHTER OF AVELINO LERENA AND AMBROSIA TRAIBEL - which places the LERENA TRAIBEL branch this archive has named since its first week. Died 2 April 1947 at Montevideo, aged NINETY-SIX. A STRONG CANDIDATE for the Josefa Lerena who stood godmother at the Catedral in 1885 and for the "Lerena de L. Josefa, 43, viuda" in Gilberto's 1895 Buenos Aires household - the ages agree within a year. DELIBERATELY NOT MERGED WITH THEM: this archive conflated two Arturo Lerenas in the same week and had to retract a branch within the hour. See data/josefa-lerena-candidates.tsv for what fits and what is untested. AND SHE MARRIED A MORATORIO, NOT A LERENA - NOW ON A PARISH ACT. Uruguayan civil registration names a Josefa Lerena as mother of Josefa, Eduardo and Maria MORATORIO LERENA, "Spouses: Fernando Moratorio", and the FamilySearch tree attaches that woman to this one. The marriage records are index-level and real; the link between the two women is a TREE ATTACHMENT and therefore SECONDARY. THE TREE ATTACHMENT IS NO LONGER NEEDED: the baptisms of her daughters MARIA CARLOTA and MARIA DE LAS MERCEDES MORATORIO LERENA at the Catedral Basilica de Montevideo on 8 March 1880 name the parents "Fernando Moratorio y Josefa Lerena" and the maternal grandparents "AVELINO y AMBROSIA TRAIBEL". So the Moratorio wife and the daughter of Avelino are one woman, on a document. AND HER CLAIM ON THE 1895 WIDOW IS WEAKER THAN THIS ROW MAKES IT SOUND, 20 September 2026. The enumerator writes wives as [maiden surname] "de L.", so his "Lerena de L." married a LERENA - and this woman married a MORATORIO, which the sentence above establishes from a parish act. Either the reading of the abbreviation is wrong, or the 1895 widow is somebody else. The two candidacies this row claims - the 1885 godmother and the 1895 widow - are not equally strong, and nothing here had said so. SHE HAS A BIRTH DAY, 21 September 2026: 10 FEBRUARY 1851. Her own baptismal act was found and read: 'bautice solemnemente a una nina a quien puse por nombre JOSEFINA LERENA, que nacio el DIEZ DE FEBRERO DEL ANO PROXIMO PASADO, hija legitima de Avelino Lerena y Ambrosia Traibel'. The act is of 1852; the archive had her year from her 1947 death registration, which is age-derived, and now has the day from the record of her christening. Godparents Don Miguel Carlos Correa de Lemus and Josefa Corbelle de Lemus; priest Domingo Ereno. AND HER NAME AT THE FONT IS JOSEFINA, NOT JOSEFA - which matters to every search that has been run on her. See data/1852-aguada-union-certificates.tsv. AND HER BAPTISM HAS A DAY TOO, within the hour: 5 DECEMBER 1852. The opening of her act sits on the previous image, 02849, where the archive had not looked: 'Jose Heimen, Cura Vicario de esta Parroquia de San Agustin en la Villa de la Union, certifica: que en el LIBRO PRIMERO DE BAUTISMOS AL FOLIO 212 se halla una partida del tenor siguiente: En cinco de Diciembre de mil...' - and the sentence finishes 'ochocientos cincuenta y dos' at the top of image 02850. So she was born 10 February 1851 and baptised at twenty-two months. Her certificate is No. 148 of seven the family lodged together in November 1896. Juan Agustin LERENA Fernandez
Connected		1791 · Canelones ("el Canelon") · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo, calle San Juan No 60
THE ELDEST, AND HE MATTERS FOR A SECOND REASON. ONE OF SANCHO'S SIX CHILDREN LISTED IN THE 1812 PADRON, and the first time any of them has been entered here. The household at calle San Juan No 60 held Sancho "andaluz" 45, Maria Fernandez 36, and six children all "naturales del CANELON" - Juan Agustin 21, Pedro Antonio 19, Agustina 17, Fidel 12, Justa 10 and Dominga 7 - together with Sancho's father-in-law RODRIGO FERNANDEZ, 63, asturiano, and four enslaved people. That all six were born at Canelones is the best support the 1791 marriage venue has, and it comes from a census rather than from a tree. NOT AN ACT: this row rests on a published transcription of a census, reported at second hand through a family tree. The act has not been opened here. THE 1812 PADRON GIVES HIM AS 21, so born about 1791 - the year of his parents' marriage. AND HE RESOLVES A MISREADING THIS ARCHIVE MADE AGAINST ITSELF. At the Montevideo baptism of 15 May 1812 the godparents are written "DON JUAN AGUSTIN SERENA y DONA AGUSTINA PERENA". This archive argued that "Juan Agustin cannot be Sancho's son, who would be a child in 1812" and talked itself out of the reading. He was twenty-one, and his sister Agustina was seventeen. The two names at that font are his and hers, and the child baptised was very likely their first cousin. So SERENA and PERENA are the machine's S-for-L again - the sixth time on this family. The tree gives his birth as 1792; the padron implies 1791. Both are kept.		no public link

Justa Pastora LERENA (daughter of Sancho)
Unplaced		Canelones · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo / Canelones
A DAUGHTER OF SANCHO LLERENA AND MARIA FERNANDEZ, and so a SISTER OF DON LUIS LERENA - found 14 September 2026 and previously unknown to this archive. Named in the Cordon baptism of her son on 13 December 1831 as "Justa Pastora Lerena, natural de CANELONES", wife of Manuel Zeferino Barros of Pernambuco. Her son's godparents were the PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC and his wife. A SECOND CHILD, October 1833, at the Catedral de la Inmaculada Concepcion - and its act names her parents FRANCISCO LERINA and Maria Fernandez, where her first child's act of 1831 said SANCHO LLERENA. She is the person through whom the alternation became visible. Her second child's godparents were ABELINO and DOMINGA LERENA. HER MARRIAGE IS NOW DATED: 3 NOVEMBER 1830, at the Cordon - to MANUEL BARROS, with her parents given as Sancho Llerena and Maria Fernandez and her birthplace as Canelones. Which puts THIRTEEN MONTHS between the wedding and the font at which FRUCTUOSO RIVERA stood godfather to her first child, in the same parish, on 13 December 1831. The index has been read and the image has not, so the date is SECONDARY; her parentage is PRIMARY from the 1831 act. AND THE ACT ITSELF IS NOW READ, not merely its index. "En tres de Noviembre de mil ochocientos treinta, precedidas informacion de libertad de MANUEL ZEFERINO BARROS natural de PERNAMBUCO, hijo legitimo de Jose [P]olanda de Barros y de Ana Maria Cabalgante, sobre el Matrimonio que libremente quiere contraer con JUSTA PASTORA LLERENA, NATURAL DE CANELONES, RESIDENTE EN EL CORDON, hija legitima de SANCHO LLERENA y de MARIA FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS." THREE GAINS IN ONE SENTENCE: she was already living at the Cordon before she married there; her mother's matronymic TRESGUERRAS is on a document for the first time; and the date is no longer index-level. The parish is DAMASO ANTONIO LARRANAGA'S - he is marrying another couple on the same page. A SECOND CHILD OF HERS IS FOUND, and her own father is written FRANCISCO on his act. AURELIO EUSEBIO BARROS LERENA, born 14 September 1833 and baptised at the Catedral on 19 October, with her own brother and sister - ABELINO LERENA and DOMINGA LERENA - as his godparents. So she has two documented children by Manuel Zeferino Barros: Juan Facundo Frutuoso (1831) and Aurelio Eusebio (1833). AND THE TWO ACTS DISAGREE ABOUT WHERE SHE WAS BORN: 1831 calls her 'natural de CANELONES', 1833 'natural de MONTEVIDEO'. Two acts, two years apart, one parish. Neither is preferred here, though Canelones has the support of her sister Dominga's baptism there in 1804. Her father is written 'Fran.co Lerenas' on the 1833 act - see the note on Sancho's row and /hypotheses/, where the SANCHO-OR-FRANCISCO question is set out in full. It is not a stray datum: it is the third Francisco attestation, and as of 21 September the only one a human has read off the plate. A THIRD CHILD, AND THE SET NOW LOOKS LIKE A SET: 21 September 2026. HILARIO, baptised at Canelones on 19 April 1835, joins JUAN FACUNDO FRUTUOSO (1831) and AURELIO EUSEBIO (1833) - three sons by Manuel Zeferino Barros at roughly two-year intervals, and at every one of the three fonts the godparents are her own brothers and sisters: Fructuoso Ribera and Bernardina Fragoso in 1831 were the exception, Abelino and Dominga Lerena stood in 1833, and Pedro Antonio Lerena and Vicenta Vega in 1835. AND THE PLACES MOVE WITH HER: Cordon in 1831, the Catedral in 1833, back to CANELONES in 1835.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Justiniana Mauricia Maxima LERENA LENGUAS
Unplaced		1843-07-30 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
IMAGE READ 13 September 2026, and her full name is JUSTINIANA MAURICIA MAXIMA - indexed merely as "Justinia". Named for her mother, and for the Capilla de la Mauricia where she was christened. Her act names the maternal grandparents as "el GENERAL D. PEDRO LENGUAS y Carmen Gonzalez" - the rank, which no other record here gives. A CONTRADICTION INSIDE THE REGISTER ITSELF, recorded 14 September 2026 and NOT resolved. Luis Eugenio's act says he was born 9 FEBRUARY 1843; his sister Justiniana Mauricia Maxima's act, the entry immediately before his on the same page in the same hand, says she was born 30 JULY 1843. That is FIVE AND A HALF MONTHS between two full siblings, and both cannot be right. Neither is a machine reading: the transcript of each act has been read verbatim, and both say "del pasado ano cuarenta y tres". THE ERROR IS IN THE SOURCE, NOT IN THIS ARCHIVE. Every act on that page is a LATER TRANSCRIPT copied out of a legajo by order of Bishop Jacinto Vera - a clerk working from loose papers three years after the events, who had already written "ano cuarenta y tres" twice on the same page. A carried-forward year is the likeliest explanation and it is a guess. Luis Eugenio's death registration of 1901 gives an age and has been read; testing it against 1843 is the next step. HER BIRTH YEAR IS SOFTER THAN IT LOOKS, 20 September 2026. "30 July 1843" comes from the phrase "del pasado ano cuarenta y tres", and every act on that opening carries the same phrase, including one for a different couple - so it is a formula written three times, not three independent dates. Hers may well be the true one; no record outside that page says so. This matters beyond her own row: her date was used on 14 September as one of three reasons to move her brother Luis Eugenio from 1843 to 1845. That conclusion still stands, but on his death act alone - the only leg external to this page.		no public link

Justo LERENA (of the Cordon)
Unplaced		Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A LERENA FATHER THIS ARCHIVE HAD NEVER MET, found 20 September 2026. Named on the burial act of his daughter Carmen at the Cementerio del Cordon on 14 August 1841: "hija legitima de Justo Lerena y de Juana Quinta[na]", read off the image. So he was a married man with a three-year-old in Montevideo in 1841, which puts his own birth somewhere around 1810 or earlier. NOTHING TIES HIM TO SANCHO. Sancho had a daughter JUSTA born about 1802 and the forename echoes; that is an echo and not an argument. The 1812 padron lists six children and no Justo, but the published child lists for that couple are demonstrably incomplete - the prose claims eight and names seven, and omits Dominga, whom a census and an act both attest - so he can be neither placed nor excluded. He was found by a search for something else entirely: a sweep of Uruguayan deaths 1815-1840 looking for Fidel.		no public link

Maria Adelina LERENA Traibel (de FEIN)
Unplaced		1865-08-13 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
THE NINTH CHILD OF AVELINO AND AMBROSIA, found 21 September 2026. Her Montevideo death registration of 20 November 1946, certificate 48, names her father AVELINO LERENA and her mother AMBROSIA TRAIBEL, her husband CARLOS ALBERTO FEIN and a son JORGE FEIN. THE BIRTH YEAR IN THIS COLUMN IS NOT A FINDING. The registration gives her birth as 1861 and her birthplace as Montevideo; the family chart gives 1865-1946. THE 1861 CANNOT EASILY BE RIGHT, and what rules it out is her own brother. Juan Arturo was born 16 February 1861 on his baptismal act, so a sister born in 1861 would have to have been born in the last six weeks of it. The year on a death registration is age-derived and is the weaker of the two, so c.1865 is entered and flagged. WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT: her own baptism, which has not been looked for. THE BIRTH YEAR IS NO LONGER A GUESS, 21 September 2026: SHE WAS BORN 13 AUGUST 1865. Her own baptismal act was found on the second opening of the same certificate book and read: 'En treinta y uno de Diciembre de mil ochocientos sesenta y cinco. El Canonigo Dn. Juan Jose Brid, bautizo solemnemente a MARIA ADELINA, NACIDA EL TRECE DE AGOSTO ULTIMO, hija legitima de Dn. Avelino Lerena y de Da. Ambrosia Traibel, Orientales. Padrinos Dn. AMBROSIO LERENA y Da. CARLOTA MUNOZ.' So the 1861 on her death registration is wrong by four years, and the reasoning that flagged it was right for the right reason - her brother Juan Arturo was born in February 1861 and no sister could be. AND A SECOND RECORD OF HER IS THE SAME ACT. FamilySearch also indexes a 'MARTA ADELINA, born 13 August 1918, christened Montevideo 9 May 1919', with godparents Ambrosio Lorena and Carlota Munoz - the same two names and the same 13 August. It is this baptism certified a second time, on a different film, and dated by the index to that certificate. One act, two films, and two index entries fifty-three years apart.		record ↗

Maria Julia Margarita Josefa Emirena Justiniana LERENA Juanico (m. HERRERA)
Unplaced		1865-10-08 · Rosario, Santa Fe · Argentine (of Uruguayan parents) · found in Rosario, then Montevideo
The only LERENA baptised at Rosario between 1731 and 1901 - and now followed to the end of the story. Born 8 October 1865, baptised at Rosario 17 February 1866 to Don Luis Lerena Lenguas and Dona Julia Juanico, "naturales del E. Oriental". On 3 November 1886, aged 21, she married Don Jaime Herrera at San Francisco de Asis, Ciudad Vieja, MONTEVIDEO - the act naming her parents again, in a second country, twenty years later. She went home. NOT the Maria Lerena who was Pablo Armando's mother: that woman had a son in February 1882, when this one was sixteen and four years from marrying someone else. HER SURNAME IS CORRECTED HERE, 14 September 2026, and an act says so. This register called her LERENA LENGUAS - which is her FATHER'S compound, not hers. Her father was Luis Lerena Lenguas; her mother was Julia Juanico; so she is LERENA JUANICO, exactly as her siblings Ema Sixta and Candido Jaime are already named on their own rows here. She was the odd one out and nothing had caught it. THE EVIDENCE IS THE ACT READ TODAY: at her sister Ema Sixta's wedding on 30 June 1913 the witnesses are written "Don Jaime Herrera y Dona Maria Perena Juanico de Herrera" - her own compound, in her own lifetime, with PERENA the machine's usual mangling of LERENA on this family. IT ALSO MATTERED FOR THE CHECKING: the compound-surname sibling test keys on the two surnames in a row's name, so while she read LERENA LENGUAS she was being grouped with her father's generation instead of with her own brothers and sisters. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, and the citation on this row needed explaining rather than correcting. Film 007713384 image 00700, folio 492, act No. 90 of the Union marriage register: 'MASSERA, Jose Pedro = con = EMMA SIXTA LERENA. El TREINTA DE JUNIO de mil novecientos TRECE ... el Presbitero Monsenor Don EUSEBIO DE LEON, con licencia del ... Iglesia Metropolitana ...' SO ACT 90 IS HER SISTER'S WEDDING, NOT HERS - which this register knew, because Ema Sixta's own row carries the same act. This row cites it because she is IN it. THE PLATE CONFIRMS IT AND CONFIRMS HER MARRIED NAME: 'Don LUIS LERENA ... FUERON TESTIGOS, do[na] ... JUANICO DE HERRERA' - she signs as a Herrera wife, which is the only thing on any plate read here that supports the '(m. HERRERA)' in her own row's title. A CITATION THAT LOOKS WRONG AND IS NOT: the act number belongs to her sister and the row is right to name it, because a witness clause is where a married woman's surname gets written down. Mauricio LERENA (of Colla/Rosario, m. Casimira Montano 1837)
Unplaced · high interest		c.1815 · Colla (Rosario, Colonia) · Uruguayan · found in San Jose de Mayo
THE PATRIARCH THIS ARCHIVE HAD RESEARCHED BUT NEVER MADE A PERSON. His marriage of 3 February 1837 at San Jose de Mayo gives birthplace COLLA - the old name of ROSARIO, COLONIA - father RAMON, mother MARIA GONZALES, wife CASIMIRA MONTANO (her parents Jose Montano and Ignacia Barredo). His children account for San Jose, Flores and Florida, with Salto and Montevideo besides - that is the finding of row 120 - and until today he existed here only inside other people's notes. TIER: INDEXED, not image-read. Not attached to Sancho's line by anything. AND THE PARENT-PAIR QUERY NAMES HIS CHILDREN, 21 September 2026. Searching MAURICIO LERENA and CASIMIRA MONTANO as a pair returns twelve Uruguayan records where either name alone returns a crowd. One is explicit: CIPRIANO LERENA MONTANA, whose 1928 death registration at Flores names both parents, and who is now in this register. FOUR MORE ARE CANDIDATES AND ARE NOT ENTERED: Manuel Lerena Montana (m. 20 April 1882, San Jose, b.1856), Ignacia Lerena (m. 27 April 1882, Trinidad, b.1864), Clara Lerena de Pallares (d. 3 March 1925, b.1848) and Ignacia Serena Leren de Dolz (d. 12 March 1921, b.1865). They match on the pair query but their own entries do not name the parents, and this archive does not enter a child on a query shape alone. The dates fit a marriage of 1837.		record ↗

Mercedes LERENA (godmother, 1880)
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
ANOTHER LERENA WOMAN, found 14 September 2026 - very likely a SISTER OF JOSEFA and so another daughter of Avelino Lerena and Ambrosia Traibel. She stood godmother with Juan Arechaga to Maria de las Mercedes Moratorio Lerena at the Catedral Basilica de Montevideo on 8 March 1880 - and the child carries her name. Nothing else in this archive names her. 'NOTHING ELSE IN THIS ARCHIVE NAMES HER' IS NO LONGER TRUE, 21 September 2026. A sister of Josefa of exactly this name is now documented: MERCEDES MICAELA LERENA TRAIBEL, born 8 May 1863, christened at the Union on 25 September 1866, daughter of Avelino Lerena and Ambrosia Traibel. THE TWO ROWS ARE NOT MERGED, AND THE REASON IS AGE. This godmother stood at a font in March 1880, when Mercedes Micaela was sixteen. That is possible and not impossible, and the surname, the parish and the family are all right - the niece is even named for her. But a sixteen-year-old godmother is an inference, and the act that would settle it is the 1880 baptism itself, whose full-text transcript this archive has read without recovering the godmother's age or parentage. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713378 image 02598, folios 411-412, Catedral Basilica de Montevideo - the two baptisms of 8 March 1880. Priest RAFAEL YEREGUI. THE TEST NAMED IN THIS ROW HAS NOW BEEN RUN, AND IT DOES NOT SETTLE ANYTHING. The row said the 1880 act 'would have to give her age or her parents' before she could be joined to Mercedes Micaela Lerena Traibel. The plate was read on 21 September 2026 and it gives NEITHER - only 'fueron padrinos JUAN [Arechaga] y MERCEDES LERENA, a quienes instruyo'. So the two rows stay apart, and the reason is now a read page rather than an unopened one. AND THE PARISH WAS WRONG, CORRECTED 21 September 2026 OFF THE PLATE. This archive placed these 1880 baptisms at the UNION, which is what the index's place field says. The act itself reads 'con licencia del infrascripto Cura Parroco DE ESTA BASILICA MENOR Y CATEDRAL DE MONTEVIDEO, bautice solemnemente EN ELLA a Maria Carlota'. They were baptised in the CATEDRAL BASILICA, not at the Union.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Named at the baptisms of Maria Carlota and Maria de las Mercedes Moratorio Lerena, Catedral Basilica de Montevideo, 8 Mar 1880 (this archive placed it at the UNION until 21 Sep 2026, on the index&#39;s place field; the act says &#39;esta Basilica menor y Catedral&#39;); ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-ZS46-J" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9BW-ZS46-J</a> - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 007713378 image 02598
Mercedes Micaela LERENA Traibel
Unplaced		1863-05-08 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
BORN 8 MAY 1863, christened at the Union on 25 September 1866 - daughter of Avelino Lerena and Ambrosia Traibel, with the paternal grandparents given as SANCHO and MARIA FERNANDEZ and the maternal as 'Dos Juan' - the machine's reading of DON JUAN Traibel - and Ysabel Centeno. Her godparents were ADOLFO DE LAPUENTE and MERCEDES MENDEZ DE LA FUENTE, for whom she is named, which is this family's habit four times over. SHE IS THE BEST CANDIDATE FOR THE MERCEDES LERENA WHO STOOD GODMOTHER AT THE UNION IN MARCH 1880 to her sister Josefa's daughter - who was in turn named Maria de las Mercedes for her. She was sixteen then, which is young and not impossible; the rows are kept apart until an act joins them. The dispensation table here records a MERCEDES LERENA who married DON LUIS P. MARQUEZ at the Catedral on 9 July 1883, identified there as this woman - and an unmarried Mercedes Lerena in 1880 is what that marriage date requires. INDEX-LEVEL: the image of her christening has not been opened. NO LONGER INDEX-LEVEL, 21 September 2026 - THE ACT ITSELF WAS FOUND AND READ. Her certificate is No. 147 in the same 1896 book that holds six of her brothers and sisters: 'ella a MERCEDES MICAELA, QUE NACIO EL OCHO DE MAYO DEL ANO MIL OCHOCIENTOS SESENTA Y TRES, hija legitima de Dn. Avelino Lerena y Da. Ambrosia Traibel, orientales. Abuelos paternos Dn. SANCHO y Da. MARIA FERNANDEZ; abuelos maternos Dn. JUAN y Da. YSABEL CENTENO. Padrinos Dn. ADOLFO DE LAPUENTE y Da. MERCEDES MENDEZ DE LA FUENTE, a quienes advirtio. Por verdad lo firmo. INOCENCIO M. YEREQUI.' The index had the date right and the archive can now say why it believes it. And 'Dos Juan' in the index is settled: the act reads 'Dn. Juan'.		record ↗

Pedro Alberto Ysabelino LERENA Traibel (the ALBERTO of 1852-1921)
Unplaced		1852-03-16 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A SON OF AVELINO LERENA AND AMBROSIA TRAIBEL THIS ARCHIVE HAD SEEN AND NEVER DATED, 21 September 2026. The 14 September working log already listed 'Pedro Alberto Isabelino' among the Lerena Traibel, off the index, and left him there. THE INDEX SAYS HE WAS BORN IN 1896, WHICH IS SIX YEARS AFTER HIS FATHER DIED. The act itself says otherwise: 'El veinte y uno de Noviembre de 1852 ... bautice solemnemente a PEDRO ALBERTO YSABELINO, que nacio el diez y seis de Marzo del mismo ano, a las once y media del dia, hijo legitimo de AVELINO LERENA y de AMBROSIA TRAIBEL'. He was born 16 MARCH 1852 and baptised at eight months. What the index dated 1896 is the CERTIFIED COPY, issued that year from libro 1o de Bautismos folio 135. HIS GODFATHER WAS HIS UNCLE: PEDRO ANT.o LERENA, with VICENTA VEGA - and the boy is named for him, as YSABELINO is for his grandmother Ysabel Centeno. Grandparents on the act: Sancho Lerena and Maria Fernandez, Juan Traibel and Ysabel Centeno. AND HE IS THE ALBERTO LERENA OF THE 1921 DEATH REGISTRATION - Montevideo, died 1 March 1921, certificate 246, father Avelino Lerena, mother Ambrosia Treibel, wife MARIA INES LARRIERA. That registration gives his age as 69 against a birth of 16 March 1852, which makes him 68 by three weeks; an age at death is the weakest field on any certificate and the identification does not rest on it. THE IDENTIFICATION IS AN INFERENCE, NOT AN ACT: no record read here writes 'Pedro Alberto Ysabelino' and 'Alberto' on the same page. What joins them is the parents, the year and the name. See data/1852-aguada-union-certificates.tsv. AND HE HAD EIGHT CHILDREN, IN A DEPARTMENT THIS BRANCH WAS NEVER KNOWN TO REACH - found 21 September 2026 by asking for him and his wife as a PAIR. Alberto Lerena with Maria Ines Larriera returns sixteen Uruguayan records, and they are eight children born in the DEPARTAMENTO DE SAN JOSE between 1879 and 1898: MARIA ADELINA (1879), ZELMIRA CARLOTA (1880), ALBERTO JULIAN (1883), CARLOS AMBROSIO FLORENCIO (bapt. 1889), MARIA CONSUELO (1892), MARIA ANTONIETA (1894), ANGEL MIGUEL AVELINO (1898) and MARIA INES, who died at Montevideo in 1922. ZELMIRA WAS ALREADY IN THIS REGISTER, entered on 14 September and written off as 'NOT ours'. She is his daughter. THREE OF THE EIGHT CARRY NAMES OUT OF THE HOUSEHOLD AT THE AGUADA - his sister Maria Adelina, his brother Carlos Ambrosio, and his father Avelino. That is corroboration and not evidence; the evidence is the death registration. All eight are INDEX-LEVEL. THE IMAGE WAS OPENED THE SAME DAY IT WAS CALLED AN ERRAND, and the weak link is now PRIMARY. Act No. 246, film 008165205 image 01604. He died at six in the morning of 1 March 1921, at the calle JUAN PAULLIER, Montevideo, and the act gives him as 'de nacionalidad ORIENTAL, NACIDO EN MONTEVIDEO, de SESENTA Y NUEVE ANOS de edad, de estado casado, de profesion RENTISTA, a consecuencia de CARCINOMA DE LA VEJIGA, segun consta del certificado del Dr. LUSSICH', and then: 'Que el finado era hijo de AVELINO LERENA, fallecido, y de AMBROSIA TREIBEL, fallecida.' BORN AT MONTEVIDEO - which is where Pedro Alberto Ysabelino was baptised, at the Aguada, and NOT where his own children were born. The declarants were not family: JUAN MIRAMONTE, fifty, of 18 de Julio, and PEDRO FERNANDEZ, forty-eight, of Cerrito 230. AND THE ACT COUNTS HIS CHILDREN: 'era casado con Maria Ines Larriera, de cuya union quedan NUEVE HIJOS llamados: Maria Adelina, Zelmira, ELEIZA, Alberto, Carlos, Maria Ines, Maria Consuelo, Maria Antonieta y Miguel Avelino.' Eight of the nine were already found in the civil index; the third is a daughter no index here has matched, and her name is not securely read - ELEIZA or ELOIZA. The age of 69 is fifteen days early against a birth of 16 March 1852, which is what an age given by two neighbours looks like. RENTISTA is a fact about this branch nothing else here records: by 1921 it lived on property income.		record ↗

Pedro Antonio LERENA (son of Sancho)
Unplaced		1793-07-31 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
ANOTHER SON OF SANCHO LLERENA AND MARIA FERNANDEZ, and so another BROTHER OF DON LUIS - found 14 September 2026. "D. PEDRO ANTONIO LERENA natural de esta", with "Abuelos Paternos: D. Sancho y Dna Maria Fernandez" named at his daughter's baptism on 4 July 1837. He married DONA VICENTA VEGA of Cerro Largo. His brother Luis and Luis's wife Carolina Oribe stood godparents to the child. HIS BAPTISM ACT IS FOUND, 15 September 2026, and it carries two things about his FATHER that nothing else did. Canelones, 1 August 1793: "El Capellan del REGIMIENTO DE INFANTERIA DE BUENOS AYRES Don Bartolome Munoz; bautizo puso oleo y crisma a PEDRO ANTONIO DE LA SSMA TRINIDAD que nacio ayer, hijo legitimo del SOLDADO DRAGON SANCHO LERENA, y de Maria Fernandez, fueron Padrinos Don Antonio Aleman, y su mujer Dona Gregoria Ribero." Born 31 July 1793 - this archive had only the year, from a tree, and an age of 19 in the 1812 padron. His full baptismal name is PEDRO ANTONIO DE LA SANTISIMA TRINIDAD. His father is called SOLDADO DRAGON - a dragoon - where every other record says only soldado. AND THE PRIEST IS THE CHAPLAIN OF THE REGIMIENTO DE INFANTERIA DE BUENOS AIRES - the exact unit whose Libros Castrenses, 1781-1814, are filmed as FamilySearch DGS 7713373. That makes the military registers the first place to look for Sancho's 1791 marriage rather than a hopeful one, because that regiment's chaplain was demonstrably serving this family at Canelones two years after it. A SECOND RECORD OF HIM, AND IT IS FIFTEEN YEARS LATER, found 21 September 2026. He stood godfather with his wife Vicenta Vega at the baptism of PEDRO ALBERTO YSABELINO LERENA at the Aguada on 21 November 1852 - his brother Avelino's son, and a boy given his own name. He was fifty-nine. Until this act the archive held him only through his daughter's baptism of 1837 and had nothing that showed him alive after it. It also shows the two brothers keeping company: Avelino asked Pedro Antonio to the font, as he and Luis had stood for each other's children at La Mauricia in 1846. Victor LERENA (d.1889, alpargatero)
Unplaced · of interest		1861 · Uruguay · Uruguayan ("oriental") · alpargatero · found in Montevideo, calle Corrales 36
A WHOLE BRANCH OF LERENAS THIS ARCHIVE HAD NEVER SEEN, found 15 September 2026 by searching FIDEL rather than the surname. His death registration: "ha fallecido VICTOR LERENA de nacionalidad oriental de veinte y ocho anos de edad, de estado soltero y de profesion ALPARGATERO, a consecuencia de TUBERCULOSIS PULMONAR ... el finado era hijo [de] PEDRO LORENA y JOSEFA SEGUEIRA, orientales, FINADOS. Abuelos paternos FIDEL LERENA y MARIA RUEDAS; maternos Jose Seguera y Ramona." He died in hospital at twenty-eight, single, of tuberculosis, and the declaration was made DE OFICIO - by the authorities, not by family. An alpargatero is a maker of rope-soled shoes. Every other Lerena this archive has placed in Montevideo is a hacendado, a juez de paz, a major or a senator's family. This is the same surname at the other end of that city. WHETHER THIS FIDEL IS SANCHO'S SON IS NOT ESTABLISHED. Sancho's Fidel was born about 1799 and the Geneanet trees give him a death in 1822, at twenty-two or twenty-three. For this to be the same man, Pedro would have to be born by 1822 and fathering Victor at about forty. That is tight but possible, and the tree's 1822 is tree-only and has never been tested against a Montevideo burial. The alternative is a second Fidel Lerena at Montevideo, which nothing rules out. Recorded as a branch to test, not as a descent. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 008165116 image 01442, folio 94 - Montevideo civil death registration No. 622 of 1889. THE ACT GIVES A CAUSE OF DEATH THE TRANSCRIPT NEVER CARRIED: 'a consecuencia de TUBERCULOSIS PULMONAR, segun consta del certificado medico'. His trade is confirmed in the priest's - the registrar's - own hand: ALPARGATERO. And the registration is 'de oficio', an official declaration rather than one made by kin, which is why no relative signs it. THE ACT RUNS TO THREE GENERATIONS: 'el finado era hijo de PEDRO LERENA y JOSEFA SEGUEIRA, orientales, FINADOS. Abuelos [paternos] FIDEL LERENA y MARIA RUEDAS: MATERNOS JOSE SEGUEIRA y RAMONA...' Zelmira Carlota LERENA LARRIERA (m. Adolfo CORDERO)
Unplaced		1880-11-04 · Departamento San Jose, Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
TESTED AND PLACED, 14 September 2026 - and she is NOT ours. Born 4 NOVEMBER 1880 in the Departamento de SAN JOSE, daughter of ALBERTO LERENA and MARIA INES LARRIERA; married ADOLFO CORDERO at Montevideo on 28 FEBRUARY 1901; died at Montevideo 4 JANUARY 1945. Children ADOLFO ANGEL FAUSTINO (b.1905, the San Jose baptism that found her), MARIA ZELMIRA (b.1903, d.1984) and MARIA EUGENIA. She was flagged because the shape was right - a Uruguayan Lerena woman married to an Argentine in the decade this archive cares about. The shape was right and the substance is not: she has a father, a birthplace and a date, and none of them touches Pablo Armando. BUT HER FATHER IS WORTH A SECOND LOOK, AND IS NOT MERGED. ALBERTO LERENA, fathering a daughter in 1880, would be the right age to be ALBERTO OCTAVIO LERENA LENGUAS - born Montevideo 22 March 1850, son of Luis and Justiniana, and so a grandson of Sancho. He would be thirty. NOTHING JOINS THEM but a forename and an age, which is precisely the evidence that produced the two Arturos. If it held, Zelmira would be a great-granddaughter of Sancho - and that is a hypothesis to test, not a line to draw. 'SHE IS NOT OURS' IS RETRACTED, 21 September 2026. SHE IS OURS. This row named the one test that would change its own verdict - who her father Alberto Lerena was - and guessed ALBERTO OCTAVIO LERENA LENGUAS. IT IS THE OTHER GRANDFATHER. Alberto Lerena's own Montevideo death registration of 2 March 1921, certificate 246, names his wife MARIA INES LARRIERA and his parents AVELINO LERENA and AMBROSIA TREIBEL. So her father is PEDRO ALBERTO YSABELINO LERENA TRAIBEL, baptised at the Aguada on 21 November 1852, and ZELMIRA IS A GREAT-GRANDDAUGHTER OF SANCHO - which is what this row said would follow if the link held. It held by a different route. THE LINK IS ONE RECORD AND IT IS INDEX-LEVEL: the 1921 image has not been opened, and that is now an errand. See data/alberto-lerena-larriera.tsv. AND THE LINK IS NO LONGER INDEX-LEVEL. Within the hour the 1921 image was opened - film 008165205 image 01604 - and it names her father's parents as AVELINO LERENA and AMBROSIA TREIBEL in his own hand-written act, and names HER among his nine children: 'Maria Adelina, ZELMIRA, Eleiza, Alberto, Carlos, Maria Ines, Maria Consuelo, Maria Antonieta y Miguel Avelino'. The chain from her to Sancho now rests on an image at every step that matters.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

EXCLUSION RE-EXAMINED 13 Sep 2026 and NARROWED. She cannot be anyone sought: arriving 1889, aged 59, seven years after Pablo Armando was born at Rosario, in a party containing no Juan Carlos and no Maria. THAT is the reason, and it rests on facts in the index. The older note also asserted that 'LERINA here is an Italian surname, not a variant of the Spanish Lerena' - a claim about the NAME rather than the people, made without opening the manifest, and of exactly the kind that got the Armando Lorena card wrong. It is withdrawn as a reason; the exclusion stands on the dates alone.		CEMLA results are not addressable — search the surname at cemla.com/buscador/

Alejandrina LLERENA (madrina, Buenos Aires 1910)
Unplaced		Argentine · found in Buenos Aires
A LLERENA WOMAN WHO SIGNED HER OWN NAME. She stood godmother at the baptism of Juan Carlos Llerena at San Nicolas, Buenos Aires in 1910, and the signature at the foot of the form is hers: 'Alejandrina Llerena'. She was invisible until the plate was opened - the machine transcript this archive worked from did not carry her. A Llerena at the font of a Llerena child is very likely kin to the father, and nothing on the act says how. Nothing else here names her.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Bartolome LLERENA
Unplaced · of interest		1824 · Cordoba · Argentine · JORNALERO (day labourer) · found in ROSARIO, Santa Fe, Seccion 2a
Still the ONLY person of this surname found in Rosario before 1882. Age 45, MARRIED, born Cordoba - so 58 in 1882. BUT the image shows him lodging among twelve unrelated individuals (washerwomen, labourers, a carpenter, a Spanish shopkeeper) with NO WIFE OR CHILDREN beside him. A day labourer, not a horseman. Surname reads LLERENA with the double L. THE ARK IS NOW RECORDED, 13 September 2026: 3:1:S3HT-XK5S-TP. Line 4 of the schedule, re-opened and checked: "Llerena / Bartolome / 45", the surname written with a DOUBLE L exactly as this archive has always read it. Note for anyone following the old citation: the film's own ?i=86 is NOT this page - the viewer's image numbering and the numbering recorded here do not agree, and ?i=86 lands on an Aguirrebarrena household with no Llerena on it. A SECOND ROW FOR HIM WAS REMOVED 20 September 2026. 'Bartolome LLERENA - Cordoba origin' was never a person: its own note read 'Cross-reference: the Rosario 1869 man was born in Cordoba' and its source read 'See the Cordoba Llerena family below'. A cross-reference is not a human being, and it was sitting in the register being counted as one. Caught by the duplicate gate on identical birth years of 1824. Dominga Josefa LLERENA Fernandez (b.1804)
Unplaced		1804-05-13 · Canelones · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A DAUGHTER OF SANCHO LLERENA AND MARIA FERNANDEZ, and now dated: born 13 May 1804 at Canelones, baptised two days later. "Una parvula de dos dias nacida, a la cual puso por nombre Dominga Josefa, hija legitima de Sancho Llerena y de Maria Fernandez; fueron los padrinos Joseph Ponteceli y Agueda Rivero, TODOS VECINOS DE LA MISMA VILLA." She is the DOMINGA LERENA who stood godmother at the Catedral de Montevideo in October 1833 to her sister Justa Pastora's child - aged twenty-nine. The archive guessed "almost certainly her sister or cousin"; she is the sister. A SISTER OF DON LUIS LERENA. A CAUTION ADDED 14 September 2026, AND NOT RESOLVED. Two indexed records name a Dominga, daughter of Sancho and Maria Fernandez, with DIFFERENT HUSBANDS: one "Dominga Lorena" married to ANTONIO RODRIGUEZ with a son Domingo (ark 1:1:6D7C-5X1Y), and one "Dominga Llarena" married to ANTONIO RUIZ DE CARVALLO with a daughter Lucinda Gregoria (ark 1:1:6DF7-HZPR). The image arks are adjacent, so the two acts are near neighbours in one register. That is either ONE WOMAN WHO MARRIED TWICE, ONE MAN WHOSE NAME IS WRITTEN TWO WAYS, or TWO SISTERS OF THE SAME FORENAME - and this archive has been caught by exactly that shape twice, with the two Arturos and the three Josefas. Both husbands being ANTONIO is suggestive and is not proof. NEITHER IMAGE OPENED; NOTHING MERGED INTO THIS ROW. See data/sancho-children-complete.tsv. Note also that a DONA DOMINGA CARVALLO stood godmother at a Lerena font in 1850, which makes the CARVALLO husband worth reading rather than assuming. THE CAUTION ABOVE IS NOW HALF ANSWERED - ON AN ACT, the same afternoon it was written. The Catedral baptism of 12 May 1827 reads: "Lucinda Gregoria del Corazon de Jesus, que nacio en veinte y cuatro de Abril ultimo, hija legitima del CAPITAN DON ANTONIO RUIZ DE CARVALLO, y de DONA DOMINGA LLERENA DE CARVALLO. Nieta Paterna del MAYOR DON JOAQUIN RUIZ DE CARVALLO y de la finada Dona Juana Suarez; MATERNA DE SANCHO LLERENA Y DE DONA MARIA FERNANDEZ." So Sancho's daughter Dominga married a CAPTAIN, son of a MAJOR - and the godfather was EXCELENTISIMO SENOR GENERAL DON FRANCISCO DE PAULA MAGGESI. A second general at a Lerena font. AND IT IDENTIFIES A WOMAN THIS ARCHIVE COULD NOT PLACE: the "DONA DOMINGA CARVALLO" who stood godmother at Alberto Octavio Lerena's baptism in 1850 is almost certainly this woman - Dominga Llerena de Carvallo, standing for her brother's son. Not merged, because the 1850 act gives her only as Carvallo; but the two rows should be read together. THE OTHER DOMINGA RECORD - the one naming a husband ANTONIO RODRIGUEZ and a son DOMINGO - IS STILL UNRESOLVED and its image is still unopened. One woman twice married, or a sister of the same name. AND THEN FULLY RESOLVED, an hour later, BY OPENING THE SECOND IMAGE. The Catedral act of 25 October 1840 reads: "bautice solemnemente a DOMINGO que nacio el cuatro de Agosto del presente ano, hijo legitimo de D. ANTONIO RODRIGUEZ DE CARBALLO, natural de la PROVINCIA DEL RIO GRANDE, y de Dona DOMINGA LERENA, natural del Estado Oriental. Nieto paterno de D. JOAQUIN RODRIGUEZ y de Dona JUANA DEL ESPIRITU SANTO, ambos naturales de la COLONIA DEL SACRAMENTO, y por parte materna de D. SANCHO LERENA y de Dona MARIA FERNANDEZ." THE TWO HUSBANDS ARE ONE MAN. "Antonio Rodriguez" and "Antonio Ruiz de Carvallo" are the same Antonio DE CARVALLO, whose first surname the registers write RUIZ in 1827 and RODRIGUEZ in 1840, and whose father is JOAQUIN in both. THERE IS ONE DOMINGA, AND SHE IS THIS ONE. Her known children are LUCINDA GREGORIA (b. 24 April 1827) and DOMINGO (b. 4 August 1840) - thirteen years apart, so there are almost certainly others between them. TWO DISCREPANCIES RECORDED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED: 1827 makes Antonio a CAPITAN and his father a MAYOR and names the mother DONA JUANA SUAREZ, "la finada"; 1840 gives no ranks, makes Antonio a native of RIO GRANDE in Brazil with parents from the COLONIA DEL SACRAMENTO, and names the mother DONA JUANA DEL ESPIRITU SANTO. A devotional epithet and a surname can belong to one woman - Juana del Espiritu Santo Suarez - and that is a reading, not a finding. AND SHE IS IN THE 1836 CENSUS, found 20 September 2026: household 114, 'Dominga Serena', 28, Montevideo, CASADA, with D. Antº R. Carballo - her husband Capitan Antonio Ruiz de Carvallo - entered Continente, casado, HACENDADO, and two unmarried daughters, LUCINDA (the Lucinda Gregoria baptised 12 May 1827) and DOMINGA CARBALLO, named for her mother. The census gives her age as 28 against her baptism of 13 May 1804, which would make her 32 - four years out, on an age a householder gave. A third daughter, JOSEFA CARBALLO, was born and lived in RIO DE JANEIRO and married Miguel Carlos Lemus of Rio at San Francisco de Asis on 24 July 1862 (ark 1:1:X9PN-PYNH) - so this family spent time in Brazil, which is ground this archive has never touched. Juan Carlos LLERENA (b. Buenos Aires 1910)
Unplaced		1910-07-16 · Buenos Aires · Argentine · found in Buenos Aires
A JUAN CARLOS LLERENA, AND HE IS NOT THE ONE. Born 16 July 1910 and baptised at the Parroquia de San Nicolas, Buenos Aires; son of RAFAEL LLERENA and MARIA CARLOTA AMADEO LASTRA. Pablo Armando Lerena was born at Rosario in 1882 and his father is named JUAN CARLOS LERENA on the enrolment card of 1908. This child was two years old in 1908. He is entered because this archive's central question is a name, and a name turns up: the useful thing a register can do is hold the ones that have been ruled out, with the reason on the row. His godmother was ALEJANDRINA LLERENA, who signed the act herself.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Maria LLERENA (Espanola, 60, head of Casa 2)
Unplaced · high interest		Spain · Spanish · found in Montevideo
A SPANISH-BORN LLERENA WOMAN OF SIXTY, KEEPING HER OWN HOUSE IN MONTEVIDEO. Entered at the head of Casa 2 as 'Maria Llerena - 60 anos - Espanola', with MANUEL, 20, 'Africana' and ROSA, 12, 'Oriental' under her, both with a ditto where a surname would go. Sancho was andaluz and 45 in 1812, so she stands in his generation or the one above it - which is the generation this archive cannot get past. ATTACHED TO HIM BY NOTHING. And the census year is NOT established, so her '60' cannot yet be turned into a birth year.		record ↗

Pedro LLERENA
Unplaced · of interest		Uruguayan · found in Colonia, Uruguay
m. Sofia Clavijo. Fathering a daughter in 1884, so precisely the generation that could have fathered a son in 1882. AND HE HAS FIVE MORE CHILDREN, found 21 September 2026 by a PARENT-PAIR QUERY. Searching PEDRO LLERENA and SOFIA CLAVIJO as a pair returns six Uruguayan civil registrations where searching either name alone returns a crowd: ISABEL (b. 14 Nov 1895), CELINA (d. 11 May 1917), TELMA (b. 2 May 1900), TOMASA CANTIDIA (b. 12 Feb 1903) and SUSANA, whom this archive already held. Four of them are indexed under the compound LLERENA CLAVIJO, which names both parents in the surname itself. Index-level; no act opened.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Argentina Cordoba Catholic Church Records
Rafael LLERENA (Buenos Aires, b. c.1881)
Unplaced · of interest		1881 · Argentina · Argentine · found in Buenos Aires, Calle Cerrito 535
AN ARGENTINE-BORN LLERENA HOUSEHOLD IN BUENOS AIRES, found 15 September 2026 and new to this archive. "bautizo solemnemente ... a JUAN CARLOS que nacio el dia diez de Julio del ano mil novecientos diez, hijo legitimo de D. RAFAEL LLERENA natural del Pais de veinte y nueve anos de edad, y de Dona MARIA CARLOTA AMADEO LASTRA natural del Pais de veinte y siete anos, domiciliados en Calle Cerrito 535." His son is called JUAN CARLOS - which is why this record surfaced at all, in a search for Pablo Armando's father. The son is NOT that man: he was born in 1910. But the FATHER is the kind of person this archive has been looking for - a Llerena born in Argentina about 1881, within a year of Pablo Armando, living in central Buenos Aires. Nothing connects him here. He is recorded as a lead, not a relative. He sits beside the other unresolved Argentine namesake already in this register, Gilberto Lerena b.1879 who landed in 1928. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, and it is a printed form, filled in, at the PARROQUIA DE SAN NICOLAS, BUENOS AIRES, ANO DE 1910. Film 004571164 image 00201, folio 370. THE CHILD IS A JUAN CARLOS. 'bautizo solemnemente a JUAN CARLOS LLERENA, que nacio el dia DIEZ Y SEIS DE JULIO del ano mil novecientos diez, hijo legitimo de D.r D. RAFAEL LLERENA, natural del PAIS, de VEINTE Y NUEVE anos de edad, y de D.a MARIA CARLOTA AMADEO LASTRA, natural del Pais, de VEINTE Y SIETE anos de edad.' So this row's estimate of c.1881 is confirmed by the act - twenty-nine in July 1910 - and the double L is his own, in a Buenos Aires parish in 1910. WHY IT MATTERS, AND WHY IT IS NOT WHAT THE ARCHIVE IS LOOKING FOR: this act creates a JUAN CARLOS LLERENA at Buenos Aires, and Juan Carlos Lerena is the name on Pablo Armando's 1908 enrolment card. This child was born in 1910. Pablo Armando was born in 1882. The child cannot be his father and is not claimed to be. He is recorded so that the next search which turns up a Juan Carlos Llerena in Buenos Aires meets a dated row rather than a fresh hope. AND THE ACT NAMES A SECOND LLERENA WOMAN: the madrina signed it herself, in her own hand, 'Alejandrina Llerena' - and a literate woman signing a Buenos Aires parish register in 1910 is the kind of person who leaves other records. The padrino signs 'A. Lastra', which is the mother's family.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
San Nicolas de Bari, Retiro, Buenos Aires, baptism of his son, 30 Aug 1910 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ, ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:939D-GD9T-LM" rel="noopener">3:1:939D-GD9T-LM</a> - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 004571164 image 00201, folio 370
Ramon LLERENA
