Uruguay Catholic Church Records - named on Gabino&#39;s marriage
Sancho LLERENA Aguilar
Unplaced		1767 · Andalusia, Spain (the 1812 padron says "andaluz"; CORDOBA comes from the trees, which cite Apolant ficha 355) · Spanish · soldado dragon (the 1793 act; "soldado" on the census) · found in Montevideo, Calle San Juan
NOW PARTLY PRIMARY. The Uruguayan census records him at Calle San Juan, Montevideo, aged 45, birthplace "ANDALUZIA", with MARIA FERNANDEZ on the line below - image opened and read. Six further indexed records name the same wife and the same children while spelling him LLERENA, LERENA, SANCIO LLERENA, D. SANCHO LERENA and LORENA. So the alternation is in the RECORDS, not in a tree. That they are all one man is still an inference, and Cordoba specifically still rests on the tree alone. ORIGINALLY RECORDED AS SECONDARY. The founder of the Montevideo Lerenas on the tree's account: a soldier from Cordoba who married Maria Josefa Fernandez Tresguerras at Canelones, 8 January 1791. His name is spelled BOTH Lerena and Llerena across forty years of Uruguayan registers, and once - in 1828 - "Sancho de Llerena". The acts have NOT been opened here. HE IS NOW ON AN ACT, 13 September 2026, and no longer only on an index and a published tree. Act 585 of 7 October 1850 names the paternal grandparents of Alberto Octavio Lerena as "D. SANCHO y Dona MARIA FERNANDEZ" - so Sancho really is the father of Don Luis Lerena, and the descent this archive marked SECONDARY is upgraded. A DISCREPANCY RECORDED AGAINST IT: two 1846 acts for other children of the same couple give the paternal grandfather as FRANCISCO, not Sancho. Those 1846 entries are LATER TRANSCRIPTS copied out of a legajo ("asi consta del Legajo conservado en este Archivo, mandado trasladar por el Sr. Obispo"), while the 1850 act is a contemporaneous parish entry - so the copy loses to the original. Everything else in both sets agrees. AND A THIRD ACT NAMES HIM, 14 September 2026 - the earliest yet. His son's marriage of 3 March 1842 gives the groom as "hijo legitimo de D. SANCHO y Dona Maria Fernandez". Two acts of 1842 and 1850 now say Sancho against two 1846 entries reading Francisco, and the 1846 pair say of themselves that they are later transcripts from a legajo. AND A FOURTH RECORD, THE EARLIEST OF ALL: his son's own marriage petition of 3 September 1836, in Luis's hand and over Luis's signature - "hijo legitimo de D. SANCHO LERENA y de Dona Maria Fernandez". Four records now name him, across 1836, 1842 and 1850. The doubt raised on 13 September by two 1846 transcripts reading "Francisco" is closed: they are copies, and they are outnumbered by originals. AND TWO MORE RECORDS, 14 September 2026, neither of them about his son Luis. (1) He is named maternal grandfather - "SANCHO LLERENA y MARIA FERNANDEZ" - in the Cordon baptism of 13 December 1831, at which the godfather was DON FRUCTUOSO RIVERA, PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC, with his wife Dona Bernardina Fragoso. (2) He appears in person as a sworn witness in a marriage investigation at Canelones: "presento por testigo en esta Vicaria a SANCHO LLERENA, a quien se le recivio juramento". Five records now name him. AND THEN THE QUESTION REOPENED, the same afternoon. The Catedral baptism of Justa Pastora's SECOND child, October 1833 - an ORIGINAL act, not a transcript - names her parents "Maternos: FRANCISCO LERINA y Maria Fernandez". So the Sancho/Francisco alternation is not simply copies against originals, which is what this archive argued an hour earlier and has now withdrawn. The one thing constant in every record is the wife, MARIA FERNANDEZ. A double forename - Sancho Francisco - would explain it all without anybody being wrong, and is the leading reading. See data/sancho-or-francisco.tsv; his own marriage act would settle it and has never been opened. AND THE "AGUILAR" IN HIS NAME HERE IS UNATTESTED. None of the eight records now assembled gives him a matronymic; a full-text search for "Llerena y Aguilar" returns thirty hits and every one is QUITO, ECUADOR - a different family. Aguilar comes from the FamilySearch Tree and from nowhere this archive has read. Kept, because the tree may rest on an act nobody here has opened, but marked. The forename is contested, the matronymic unsupported, and MARIA FERNANDEZ is the only part of this man's identity every record agrees on. AND HE IS NOW ATTESTED IN 1804, twenty-seven years earlier than anything this archive held two days ago. At Canelones on 15 May 1804 his daughter DOMINGA JOSEFA is baptised "hija legitima de SANCHO LLERENA y de MARIA FERNANDEZ"; in the confirmation list of the same year he stands godfather to "Jose, ESCLAVO DE MANUEL LOPEZ"; and in the marriage-investigation files he gives evidence under oath. Father, godfather and sworn witness, in one town, in one decade - and every one of those acts calls him SANCHO. The machine transcript renders him SANCIO on the baptism, a sixth spelling for this family. AND THEN BACK ANOTHER FIFTEEN YEARS, on 14 September 2026: HE IS IN MONTEVIDEO IN 1789. At the Iglesia Matriz on 30 June 1789 the godparents of Juana, daughter of "NICOLAS BENTENO, INDIO NATURAL DE LA CIUDAD DEL TUCUMAN, y de Leonarda Correa de Buenos Ayres", are "SANCHO LLERENA y Juliana Josefa Cornejo". He is about twenty-two, unmarried, and in Montevideo two years before the marriage his tree places at Canelones in 1791. And it is the second time this archive has found him sponsoring at the margins of colonial society: in 1804 he stood godfather to an ENSLAVED MAN, and here to the daughter of an indigenous man from Tucuman. Twice, fifteen years apart. THE TALLY IS ALSO REDONE, AND THE ARCHIVE HAD BEEN COUNTING IT WRONG. Fourteen records now name him: ten SANCHO, four FRANCISCO. But THREE OF THE FOUR FRANCISCOS ARE THE SAME PAGE - La Mauricia, 7 January 1846, one clerk writing three acts on one morning out of one legajo. They are one witness, not three. The Sanchos come from nine separate occasions across sixty-one years, in four parishes and two towns, and twice with the man himself present. So the evidence is not ten to four. It is nine independent occasions against two, and both of the two describe him at second hand by clerks who never met him. A DOUBLE FORENAME REMAINS THE LEADING READING and explains everything without anyone being wrong - but this archive can no longer call the question evenly balanced. AND HIS WIFE'S FULL NAME IS NOW ON AN ACT: her daughter's marriage of 3 November 1830 calls her "MARIA FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS". This archive carried that matronymic from a PUBLISHED TREE and marked it secondary; the tree was right, about something no index gives. Which is a point in favour of that same tree's other unverified claim - that they married at CANELONES on 8 JANUARY 1791. That act has still never been opened. And 'AGUILAR' is STILL unattested across all fourteen records. AND ON 14 SEPTEMBER 2026, GENEANET SUPPLIES THE ONE THING THIS ROW HAS BEEN CALLING UNATTESTED ALL DAY. Two Spanish archive indexes - the Index des mariages catholique de Grenade and Spain, Marriages 1565-1950 - carry a marriage of SANCHO LERENA y ARMENTA to ANA MARIA DE AGUILAR, in the DIOCESE OF GRANADA, between 1725 and 1775. A son of that couple would be styled LERENA y AGUILAR - which is exactly the name the trees give this man. So the matronymic is not a tree's invention but a real Spanish naming rule, and it points at a GRANDMOTHER. NOT ADOPTED: the index has been read and the image has not, the date range is a fifty-year span, and no record yet joins that couple to a son born in 1767. But it turns an unattested word into a testable claim in a named diocese, and it is the first thing this archive has ever had that could document this family in SPAIN. GRANADA also sits against the trees' CORDOBA - one of them, ckurti, says ZUHEROS, CORDOBA - while the census says only ANDALUZIA, which both satisfy. AND FOUR TREES GIVE HIM A DEATH IN 1832, where this archive has no death date at all; if true, his son's marriage petition of 1836 names a dead man, which is ordinary. Tree-only, and recorded as a thing to test against Montevideo burials. AND ON 14 SEPTEMBER 2026 THE GRANADA ACT ITSELF WAS FOUND - BUT READ THE NEXT SENTENCE BEFORE CREDITING THIS ROW WITH IT. The birthplaces below belong to a man in SPAIN in 1754, NOT to the Sancho of this row, and nothing yet joins the two. This archive very nearly wrote the Spanish Cordoba into the Uruguayan Sancho's birthplace field on the strength of it; the field above still says CORDOBA COMES ONLY FROM A TREE, and that is still true of THIS man. FamilySearch ark 1:1:C9WG-BGW2 indexes the marriage of SANCHO DE LERENA Y ARMENTA to ANA MARIA DE AGUILAR at SANTA ANA, GRANADA on 17 NOVEMBER 1754 - the fifty-year span reduced to a day - and it gives the groom's BIRTHPLACE AS CORDOBA and his parents as SANCHO DE LERENA Y VELASCO and MARIA ANTONIA DE ARMENTA Y GUEBARA , with the bride the daughter of FRANCISCO DE AGUILAR and BEATRIS FRANCO of La Malaha. That resolves a tension in the SPANISH record - the Geneanet trees said CORDOBA and the marriage index said GRANADA, and both were right, because he was born at one and married at the other. It does NOT reach across the Atlantic. The Uruguayan census's "ANDALUZIA" for the man in this row remains the only birthplace evidence this archive has for HIM, and it is compatible with Cordoba, Granada and much else besides. The image is marked IMAGE UNAVAILABLE and has NOT been opened. AND THE COMPOUND IS NO LONGER A TREE'S HABIT. A Madrid notarial act of 27 March 1790 (ark 3:1:3Q9M-CS2C-N7RV) is signed, in the signature position between the witnesses and the notary, "JUAN DE LERENA Y AGUILAR" - one man, that compound, in the right family and generation, in a Madrid where Sancho de Lerena y Armenta was a vecino from 1766 to 1808. BUT NOT ONE RECORD JOINS ANY OF THEM TO THIS MAN. A controlled search for children of that couple in Spain returned a genuine null (control: 12 results), and the exact-phrase search for "Sancho de Lerena y Aguilar" returned an UNCONTROLLED "(0)" immediately before FamilySearch blocked the session - which is not evidence and is not recorded as a null. So "Aguilar" stays exactly where it was: a strong lead with a Spanish family behind it now instead of nothing, and still unattested in all fourteen Uruguayan records. The place to look for a 1767 baptism is MADRID, not Granada - the earlier errand said "the same diocese" and was written before the Madrid residence was known. See data/1754-granada-act.tsv. AND THEN, THE SAME DAY, THE GRANADA READING WAS WITHDRAWN - BY THE VERY TREE THAT SUPPLIES "AGUILAR". The jfina Geneanet tree, one of the four this archive cites for the form "Lerena y Aguilar", gives this man's parents as FELIX LERENA and ROSALIA AGUILAR, born Cordoba about 1767, citing APOLANT, "Operativo Patagonia" (Montevideo 1970), FICHA 355. So AGUILAR is his MOTHER'S SURNAME - ordinary Spanish naming - and not a grandmother from Granada. The morning's inference, that the compound must descend from the 1754 Granada couple, was a naming rule applied to a name whose actual owner is recorded elsewhere; and the controlled null against that couple is explained rather than puzzling. The Spanish search that matters now is FELIX LERENA and ROSALIA AGUILAR at CORDOBA, which is not the search that was run. AND THE MARRIAGE DATE FINALLY HAS A REAL SOURCE WHILE THE PLACE LOSES ITS ONE: 8 January 1791 comes from Apolant ficha 355, a named published work; "Canelones" appears nowhere in it - the note's "Concejo de Morcin, Asturias" is the BRIDE'S birthplace, she being JOSEFA FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS b.1778 at Morcin, an ASTURIAN of the Operativo Patagonia convoy, daughter of RODRIGO FERNANDEZ TRESGUERRAS and ROSA FERNANDEZ. Canelones remains the strong inference because the 1812 padron lists all six children as "naturales del Canelon", the eldest 21. THE 1812 PADRON ALSO HOLDS WHAT THIS ROW DID NOT: calle San Juan No 60, Sancho "andaluz" 45, Maria Fernandez 36, children Juan Agustin 21, Pedro Antonio 19, Agustina 17, Fidel 12, Justa 10 and DOMINGA 7 - all "naturales del Canelon" - his father-in-law RODRIGO FERNANDEZ, 63, asturiano, living in the house, and FOUR ENSLAVED PEOPLE (AGN, Fondo ex AGA, L 251). See data/apolant-ficha-355.tsv. AND ON 15 SEPTEMBER 2026 HIS PARENTS ARRIVED ON AN ACT, WHICH CLOSES THE LAST WEAK JOINT IN THIS ROW. At the Iglesia Matriz of Montevideo on 10 February 1811, at the baptism of his son LUIS ESCOLASTICO, the act names him "Don SANCHO LLERENA natural de ANDALUCIA" and his parents "Abuelos paternos FELIZ GERENA y ROSALIA AGUILAR" - GERENA being the machine's G-for-L, a seventh variant for this family. Until that morning FELIX LERENA and ROSALIA AGUILAR rested on ONE un-consulted journal article reported at second hand through one tree, and this row said so. They are now on a parish act written while he was alive and standing at his own son's font. ANDALUCIA IS ALSO NOW ON AN ACT and no longer only on the 1726-1858 census. CORDOBA IS STILL ON NO ACT and remains what the trees say, citing Apolant ficha 355. AND THE TALLY OF HIS SPONSORSHIPS IS WRONG IN THIS ROW AND IS CORRECTED, 15 September 2026. It says he stood godfather at the margins of colonial society "twice, fifteen years apart" - the indigenous man's daughter in 1789 and an enslaved man in 1804. It is at least FOUR times, and three of them on one day. Three further pages of the 1804 Canelones confirmations name him as padrino, including "Joseph Jimenez ESCLAVO DE DON LUIS JIMENEZ. P. Sancho Llerena" - a different enslaved man with a different owner from the Jose, esclavo de Manuel Lopez, already recorded here. The pattern is now too consistent to read as chance. A NEW CHILD IS ALSO ADDED: JUSTA PASTORA, baptised at Canelones on 9 August 1800, born three days earlier - this archive had her only from her 1830 marriage and from the padron's rough age of 10. That act transcribes the mother as 'MARIA RODRIGO', which is not her name in any of the other fourteen records. The most economical reading is that a clerk wrote her father's forename - RODRIGO Fernandez - and that only became visible once the 1811 act named him. The image has not been opened and the discrepancy is left standing. AND ON 15 SEPTEMBER 2026 THREE MORE ACTS ARRIVED, BECAUSE THE NAME WAS FINALLY SEARCHED WITH ONE L. Every full-text sweep here had been run on SANCHO LLERENA. "Sancho LERENA" returns three Canelones baptisms that were not among them. HIS RANK: the 1793 act calls him "el SOLDADO DRAGON Sancho Lerena" - a dragoon, where the census says only soldado. HIS REGIMENT, BY IMPLICATION: that act was performed by "el Capellan del REGIMIENTO DE INFANTERIA DE BUENOS AYRES Don Bartolome Munoz" - the same regiment whose Libros Castrenses of 1781-1814 are filmed as DGS 7713373. His 1791 marriage may well be in those military books, and the reason to look is now documented rather than hopeful. HIS WIFE IS CALLED TERESA: at a Canelones baptism of 27 October 1805 the godparents are "SANCHO LERENA y MARIA TERESA FERNANDEZ de este Pueblo" - the trees give her as Maria Josefa (TERESA) Fernandez Tresguerras and this archive had marked those forenames tree-only. AND THE SPONSORSHIP COUNT RISES AGAIN, to five. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT since 14 September. Film 007713375 image 02410, Iglesia Matriz de Montevideo, the baptism of Luis Escolastico, 10 February 1811 - the only record in this archive that names Sancho's own parents. 'hijo legitimo de D.n SANCHO LLERENA nat.l de ANDALUCIA' - read off the plate, not off a transcript. The double L is the priest's own, in 1811. THE 1833 ACT THAT CALLS HIM FRANCISCO WAS READ OFF THE PLATE, 21 September 2026 - and that is the whole of what this archive gained today on the question. Film 007713376 images 01176-01177: the Catedral baptism of 19 October 1833, for Justa Pastora's son Aurelio Eusebio, gives the maternal grandparents as 'Fran.co Lerenas y M.a Fernandez'. Until today that reading was a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT - the page and data/sancho-or-francisco.tsv both record it as 'FRANCISCO LERINA', machine-read. A human has now read it off the plate, so the single most important FRANCISCO attestation is no longer possibly a machine artefact. THIS ROW SAID SOMETHING ELSE FOR ABOUT AN HOUR AND IT WAS WRONG. It called the Francisco a priest's slip, said 'every other record this archive has read off a plate calls him SANCHO', and put the count at 'one act against five'. All three are false, and the archive's own file and /hypotheses/ already said so: the 1846 La Mauricia acts were READ OFF THE IMAGE by this archive and say FRANCISCO; the count is SIX saying Sancho against THREE saying Francisco; and the readings split ACROSS methods, not along them - the machine read Sancho in 1831 and Francisco in 1833, this archive read Francisco off 1846 and Sancho off 1836, 1842 and 1850. THE ARCHIVE'S POSITION, WHICH THIS ROW NOW MATCHES: a DOUBLE FORENAME - Sancho Francisco or Francisco Sancho - is the reading that requires nobody to be wrong. What is constant across seventy years and both forenames is the wife: MARIA FERNANDEZ, in every single record. WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT is his own marriage act, said by the tree to be Canelones, 8 January 1791, and never opened.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Agustin LORENA
Unplaced		1829 · Lima, PERU · Peruvian · OJALATERO (tinsmith) · found in Rosario, Santa Fe

ARMAND[O] LORENA (as machine-indexed) = Pablo Armando LERENA
Connected		1882-02-22 · Rosario, Santa Fe · Argentine · found in Argentina
RESOLVED - THIS IS PABLO ARMANDO HIMSELF. The image was opened on 10 Sep 2026 and the card reads 'Clase de 1882 / LERENA - Pablo Armando / hijo de Juan Carlos Lerena y de Maria Lerena / Nacido en Rosario de Santa Fe / Oficina enroladora de CAPETOWN'. The machine index had dropped 'Pablo', moved him 1,400 km to Salta, turned Lerena into Lorena, and made a second surname 'Serena' out of the printed word SERIE on the next line. Every one of the four objections logged against this candidate was an artefact of the transcription. Kept in the register under its indexed name so the false trail stays visible.		record ↗

Pedro LORENA (father of Victor, d. before 1889)
Unplaced · of interest		Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Named on his son's death registration of 1889 as already dead - "el finado era hijo [de] PEDRO LORENA y JOSEFA SEGUEIRA, orientales, FINADOS". His own parents are given: FIDEL LERENA and MARIA RUEDAS. The surname is written LORENA for him and LERENA for his son and his father in the same sentence, which is this family's ordinary behaviour in the records. WHETHER THIS FIDEL IS SANCHO'S SON IS NOT ESTABLISHED. Sancho's Fidel was born about 1799 and the Geneanet trees give him a death in 1822, at twenty-two or twenty-three. For this to be the same man, Pedro would have to be born by 1822 and fathering Victor at about forty. That is tight but possible, and the tree's 1822 is tree-only and has never been tested against a Montevideo burial. The alternative is a second Fidel Lerena at Montevideo, which nothing rules out. Recorded as a branch to test, not as a descent. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 008165116 image 01442, folio 94 - Montevideo civil death registration No. 622 of 1889. THIS ROW'S CLAIM ABOUT THE SPELLING IS WRONG, AND THE PLATE SHOWS IT. The row said the surname 'is written LORENA for him and LERENA for his son and his father in the same sentence'. In the body of the act he is written LERENA, exactly like his son and his father: 'era hijo de PEDRO LERENA y Josefa Segueira'. LORENA is the MARGIN heading - 'Num. 622 Victor Lorena' - which is the clerk's index entry, not the sentence. So the family's ordinary confusion of L and Ll and Lor is real, but this particular instance of it was the archive reading a margin as though it were the text. Exmo Sr General Don Francisco de Paula MAGGESI
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
GODFATHER at the Catedral de Montevideo on 12 May 1827 to Lucinda Gregoria Ruiz de Carvallo, a granddaughter of Sancho Llerena. A general, styled Excelentisimo Senor. This archive now has FRUCTUOSO RIVERA at a font in 1831, an ORIBE bride in 1836, LAVALLEJA godparents in 1858, GENERAL PEDRO LENGUAS as a grandfather, and GENERAL MAGGESI here in 1827. No relationship to any of them is claimed; what is recorded is the company this family kept at its christenings. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713376 image 00601, folio 150 - the baptism of 12 May 1827 at the Iglesia Matriz de San Felipe y Santiago de Montevideo. THE THIRD FAMOUS NAME THIS ARCHIVE CHECKED TODAY, AND THE THIRD TO HOLD. The act reads 'Fueron padrinos EL EXMO. SR. GRAL. [Don Francisco de Pa]ULA MAGGESI y D.a Carolina Amalia'. A machine that meets a name out of the history books will write it whether or not the page says so - that is how FRUCTUOSO RIVERA got printed over the 1831 act's own FRUTUSO RIBERA. Here, as with the Lavallejas of 1858, the page really does say it.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

MARIA MERCEDES (daughter of Juana, enslaved)
Connected		1812-09-24 · Montevideo · found in Montevideo
BORN INTO SLAVERY IN THIS FAMILY'S HOUSEHOLD, 24 September 1812. "bautice solemnemente a MARIA MERCEDES, que nacio antes de ayer, NEGRA ESCLAVA DE DON SANCHO LERENA Y DE DONA MARIA FERNANDEZ, hija de JUANA. Madrina Catalina Martinez." She was born in the same month the padron was taken that counted four enslaved people in that house and named none of them. Nothing is known of what became of her. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713375 image 02628, folio 136, Montevideo Catedral - the baptism of 26 September 1812. READ OFF THE PLATE, and every word of this row holds: 'bautice solemnemente a MARIA MERCEDES, que nacio ANTES DE AYER, hija de JUANA, negra esclava de D.n Sancho Lerena y de D.a Maria Fernandez: MADRINA CATALINA MARTINEZ, a quien instrui; y por verdad lo firme.' Born 24 September 1812, baptised at two days old. AND THE PLATE CARRIES A TRAP THIS ARCHIVE WALKED PAST. The act IMMEDIATELY BEFORE hers, on the same opening, baptises ANOTHER MARIA MERCEDES on 25 September 1812 - 'hija de TERESA ABELLEYRA, negra esclava de D.n RAMON ABELLEYRA'. Two enslaved girls of the same name, in two households, on consecutive days, on one page. This register also holds a TERESA enslaved in Sancho's household, from the padron - a different woman, from a different document, and the two must not be run together. She is entered because she is the only person the record puts beside Maria Mercedes who was not her owner, and because this register names the godparents of every other child on its pages. WHO SHE WAS IS NOT KNOWN: the act gives her no title - no Dona - which in this book usually marks a woman who is not of the propertied class, and it gives her no husband, no origin and no relationship to the household. Maria Carlota MORATORIO Lerena
Unplaced		1880-02-24 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Daughter of Fernando Moratorio and Josefa Lerena Traibel, baptised at the Catedral Basilica de Montevideo on 8 March 1880 with her sister. "PROBABLY TWINS" IS AN UNTESTED INFERENCE AND IS FLAGGED AS ONE, 20 September 2026. It rests on nothing but a shared baptism date, and that reasoning was disproved elsewhere in this family on the same day: Ana Rosario and Alfredo Justiniano Lerena Traibel were baptised together at San Francisco de Asis in 1858 and were born twenty-one months apart. The test is cheap and unrun: the full-text transcript of these two acts should state each girl's own birth date, as the 1858 pair do. A granddaughter of Avelino Lerena. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 007713378 image 02598, folios 411-412, Catedral Basilica de Montevideo - the two baptisms of 8 March 1880. Priest RAFAEL YEREGUI. THE TEST THIS ROW NAMED AS CHEAP AND UNRUN HAS BEEN RUN, AND 'PROBABLY TWINS' IS CONFIRMED. The act states her own birth: 'que nacio el VEINTE Y CUATRO DE FEBRERO ultimo'. Her sister's act, on the facing folio, states the same day. THEY ARE TWINS, born 24 FEBRUARY 1880 and carried to the font twelve days later. Her godparents were FRANCISCO AZANOLA and MANUELA OTONDO - not, as this archive half-assumed, the same pair as her sister's. The act also gives the paternal grandparents FERNANDO and NICOLASA TORRES and the maternal AVELINO and AMBROSIA TRAIBEL, and calls the parents 'orientales'. AND THE PARISH WAS WRONG, CORRECTED 21 September 2026 OFF THE PLATE. This archive placed these 1880 baptisms at the UNION, which is what the index's place field says. The act itself reads 'con licencia del infrascripto Cura Parroco DE ESTA BASILICA MENOR Y CATEDRAL DE MONTEVIDEO, bautice solemnemente EN ELLA a Maria Carlota'. They were baptised in the CATEDRAL BASILICA, not at the Union.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

SHE AND HER SISTER WERE BOTH BORN 24 FEBRUARY 1880 - read off both acts. 'que nacio el veinte y cuatro de Febrero ultimo, hija legitima de Fernando Moratorio y Josefa Le[rena] ... fueron padrinos JUAN [Arechaga y Mercedes] LERENA'. So the twins reading is right here and was wrong in 1858, where Ana Rosario and Alfredo Justiniano were carried to the same font twenty-one months apart. Same family, same habit of batch-baptism, and the shared date means something in one case and nothing in the other - which is exactly why it had to be read rather than assumed. Dona Carolina ORIBE (1st wife of Don Luis Lerena)
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
HER SURNAME IS ORIBE, AND THIS ARCHIVE RECORDED IT WRONG TWICE. She was written here as Carolina OLIVA, from the 1836 marriage petition and from FamilySearch's index of it - which says Oliva, and which is where the reading came from. The Catedral baptism of 4 July 1837, a different book in a different hand, names her "Dna CAROLINA ORIBE" standing godmother with her husband to his brother's child. The 1836 petition was then re-opened and magnified: it reads ORIBE there too, with a clear b. And the published genealogy this archive has always tiered as SECONDARY said all along that Don Luis married firstly a daughter of an ORIBE. It was right. She married Luis Lerena at Montevideo in September 1836, was ALIVE IN JULY 1837, and was dead by March 1842 when he remarried "en segundas nupcias". ORIBE is one of the great Uruguayan names - Manuel Oribe was President 1835-1838 and founder of the Blancos - but NOTHING READ HERE MAKES HER HIS KIN. Oribe is a surname, not a proof, and no record yet names her parents. PLACE FIELD CORRECTED 14 September 2026. This row's 'where found' held a CITATION - an empty placeholder - where the atlas expects a PLACE, so this person was missing from the map and appeared on it as a phantom location instead. The citation is kept here; the field now holds a real place.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry
Marriage petition of 3 September 1836, Arquidiocesis de Montevideo - IMAGE READ; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-J331-G" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-J331-G</a>; and named as his deceased first wife in the act of 3 March 1842; and named with her husband as godmother in the Catedral baptism of 4 July 1837 - ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-YSKH-H" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-C9B7-YSKH-H</a>
Dr. D. Juan Ciriaco OTAEGUI (priest, 1833)
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · presbitero · found in Montevideo
The priest who performed the baptism of Aurelio Eusebio Barros Lerena at the Catedral de Montevideo on 19 October 1833 and signed the act. This archive names the priest of every act it has read off a plate - Martin Perez in 1858, Juan Lloberas in 1811, Benito Alonso Covian in 1831, Ramon de Barbera in 1837, Rafael Yeregui in 1880 - because a signature dates and authenticates an act, and because a priest's hand is what a machine transcript is least able to read. Jose (esclavo de Manuel Lopez, confirmed 1804)
Unplaced		Canelones · Uruguayan · found in Canelones
Confirmed at Canelones in 1804 with SANCHO LLERENA as his godfather. The act names him as this archive's sources name the enslaved of that place and time - a forename and an owner, "Jose, esclavo de Manuel Lopez" - and he is entered here under it because leaving him out would be a second erasure. Slavery was legal in the Banda Oriental in 1804. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. Film 008165032 image 00505 - the Canelones confirmation list of 1804, a page of one-line entries. HIS LINE SURVIVES THE CHECK WORD FOR WORD: 'JOSE ESCLAVO DE MAN.L LOPEZ PADRINO SANCHO LLERENA'. It is one line on a page of one-line entries, and it is the whole of what any record gives him. SANCHO IS SPELLED LLERENA HERE, with the double L - as in the 1811 and 1831 acts, and against the LERENA of 1837 and 1858. The entries around him have the same shape - a forename, a parent or an owner, a godfather - so the form of his entry is the register's, not a slight peculiar to him. What is peculiar to him is that where the others are named 'hijo de', he is named 'esclavo de'.		no public link
Canelones confirmation records 1804 - FULL-TEXT TRANSCRIPT READ; ark <a class="ark" href="https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS2W-73LL-2" rel="noopener">3:1:3Q9M-CS2W-73LL-2</a> - IMAGE READ 21 September 2026; film 008165032 image 00505
Josefa in Gilberto's 1895 household (b. c.1852)
Unplaced		c.1852 · Uruguay · Uruguayan · found in Buenos Aires
ALMOST CERTAINLY THE JOSEFA LERENA WHO STOOD GODMOTHER TO GILBERTO'S SON RAUL at the Catedral de Montevideo on 10 September 1885 - a woman this archive met and could not place. The 1895 census finds her in Gilberto's own Buenos Aires household: "Lerena de L. Josefa, m, 43, VIUDA, R. Oriental". Born about 1852, so of his generation. THE FORM OF HER NAME IS 'LERENA DE L.' On the two lines above her the enumerator writes married women as [maiden surname] de L. - Lenguas de Lerena, Salvanach de Lerena. If he is consistent, SHE WAS BORN A LERENA AND MARRIED A LERENA. That is the exact pattern behind this archive's standing question - whether Maria Lerena, named on Pablo Armando's enrolment card, was a Lerena by birth. Stated as a reading of an abbreviation in an enumerator's hand, not as a fact: her own marriage record would settle it and has not been looked for. CORRECTED WITHIN THE HOUR: THE 'DE LERENA' READING IS WITHDRAWN AS EVIDENCE. Uruguayan civil registration carries a JOSEFA LERENA married to FERNANDO MORATORIO, and FamilySearch's tree attaches her to Josefa Lerena Traibel 1851-1947 - the same candidate. If that is this woman, "de L." is the enumerator's shorthand for the LERENA HOUSEHOLD rather than her husband's surname, and this entry says nothing about a Lerena-Lerena marriage. The letter was read right; the inference was not. See data/josefa-lerena-candidates.tsv. AND THE WITHDRAWAL IS NOW ON A DOCUMENT RATHER THAN A TREE. The 1880 baptisms of Josefa Lerena's daughters name her husband Fernando Moratorio and her parents Avelino and Ambrosia Traibel. If this census woman is that Josefa - she is 43 here against a birth of 1851, and a VIUDA where a later record calls Fernando Moratorio "fallecido" - then "de L." is the enumerator's shorthand for the LERENA HOUSEHOLD and nothing more. Joseph PONTECELI
Unplaced		Canelones · Uruguayan · found in Canelones
Godfather at the baptism of Dominga Josefa Llerena, Canelones, 15 May 1804. "Vecino de la misma Villa." THIS ROW'S SOURCE AND ITS NOTE DISAGREE, FOUND 21 September 2026 BY OPENING THE PLATE. The note says this person stood godparent at the BAPTISM of Dominga Josefa Llerena, Canelones, 15 May 1804. The source field cites ark 3:1:3Q9M-CS2W-73LL-2, which is the CANELONES CONFIRMATION LIST - a page of one-line entries, opened and read on 21 September, on which this name does not appear in the portion read. Dominga Josefa's own row cites a DIFFERENT ark for her baptism, 3:1:3Q9M-CS2Z-W9V2-Y - film 008166571 image 01130, Nuestra Senora de Guadalupe, Canelones, 1804, which was also opened and is the right parish and the right year. THE LIKELIEST READING IS THAT THE ARK ON THIS ROW IS SIMPLY WRONG - the two were recorded in one sitting off one search and the citation was crossed - but it is NOT ASSUMED, because the alternative is that the note is wrong and this person is a confirmation godparent. NEITHER IS WRITTEN AS FACT. TEST: read film 008166571 image 01130 for the act of 15 May 1804 and see who signs as godparent. THE CONFLICT FLAGGED ON THIS ROW THIS MORNING IS RESOLVED, AND THE ARK WAS THE THING THAT WAS WRONG. IMAGE READ 21 September 2026: film 008166571 image 01130 - the Canelones baptism register, where the act reads: 'de los CANELONES bautizo en [ella a una parvula de ... ] dias nacida, a la cual puso [por nombre DOMINGA ...], legitima de SANCHO LLERENA [y Maria Fernandez]. Padrinos JOSEF PONTECELI y [Agueda Rivero], de la misma Villa, a quienes [advirtio la obligacion] ... el Cura y Vic.o propio de la [misma] ... certifico para q.e conste.' So the note was right and the source field was wrong: this person is a godparent at the BAPTISM of Dominga Josefa Llerena, and the ark this row carried - 3:1:3Q9M-CS2W-73LL-2 - is the CANELONES CONFIRMATION LIST, a different film entirely. The ark is corrected to Dominga's own, 3:1:3Q9M-CS2Z-W9V2-Y, which resolves to this image. Two records taken down in one sitting off one search, and the citations crossed. Don Fidel REY
Unplaced		Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
Godfather to Sancho's daughter Agustina Ramona at Canelones on 30 August 1795, and expressly 'VECINO DE MONTEVIDEO' where the parents are 'vecinos de la Villa'. He is very likely the reason this family uses the forename FIDEL: Sancho's son FIDEL was born about 1799, four years after this man stood at his sister's font, and the naming-for-the-godparent pattern holds in five other acts read here. THAT IS AN INFERENCE FROM A PATTERN AND NOT FROM A RECORD. Like Jose Martinez de la Vega at the 1806 font, he is a godparent fetched from the capital to a country parish - a connection of some kind, and the act does not say what kind. Don Fructuoso RIVERA
Unplaced		Uruguay · Uruguayan · President of Uruguay · found in Montevideo / Canelones
PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC OF URUGUAY, 1830-1834 - and godfather, with his wife Dona Bernardina Fragoso, to Juan Facundo Frutuoso Barros Lerena at the Cordon on 13 December 1831. The child was a grandson of Sancho Llerena. Padrinos of that rank are not chosen at random, and this is the earliest indication this archive has of the family's standing. THE PLATE WAS OPENED 21 September 2026 AND HE IS ON IT - with his office, and spelled the priest's way, not the history books'. The act reads 'padrinos Dn. FRUTUSO RIBERA, PRESIDENTE DE LA REPUBLICA y su esposa Da. BERNARDINA FRAGOSO'. FRUTUSO RIBERA, with a B. Everything published here until today came from a MACHINE full-text transcript, which printed the famous spelling FRUCTUOSO RIVERA over what the page actually says. The identification is not weakened by that - it is strengthened, because the act states his OFFICE and names his WIFE, and in December 1831 the President of the Republic was Fructuoso Rivera and his wife was Bernardina Fragoso. Two independent facts on one line, either of which identifies him alone.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Domingo RODRIGUEZ DE CARVALLO
Unplaced		1840-08-04 · Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
A GRANDSON OF SANCHO, born 4 August 1840 and baptised at the Catedral de Montevideo on 25 October. Son of D. Antonio Rodriguez de Carballo and Dona Dominga Lerena, and so a younger brother of Lucinda Gregoria (b.1827). He is the child whose existence made the two-Dominga problem, and the act that names him is the one that solved it. Godparents Don Joaquin Sosa and Dona Balvina Suarez. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, and every reading holds. Film 007713376 image 01689, margin 'Domingo Rodrig.z de Carvallo': 'En VEINTE Y CINCO DE OCTUBRE de mil ochoc.s CUARENTA ... bautice solemnem.te a DOMINGO que NACIO EL CUATRO DE AGOSTO DEL PPTE. ANO, hijo legitimo de D. ANTONIO RODRIGUEZ DE CARBALLO, nat.l de la PROVINCIA DEL RIO [de la Plata], y de D.a DOMINGA LERENA, nat.l del ESTADO ORIENTAL. Nieto pat[erno de D. Domingo] RODRIG.Z y D.a JUANA DEL ESPIRITU S.TO, AMBOS NAT.S DE LA COLONIA; por parte Mat.a de D. SANCHO LERENA y D.a M.a FERNANDEZ.' THREE THINGS THE TRANSCRIPT DID NOT CARRY INTO THIS REGISTER: his father is natural de la PROVINCIA DEL RIO and not of Montevideo; his paternal grandparents are DOMINGO RODRIGUEZ and JUANA DEL ESPIRITU SANTO, 'ambos naturales de la COLONIA'; and the act spells the father CARBALLO with a B where this register writes CARVALLO. THE MATERNAL SIDE IS SANCHO LERENA AND MARIA FERNANDEZ, named again - the seventh plate read today to say so.		FamilySearch index — ark not recorded for this entry

Capitan Don Antonio RUIZ [RODRIGUEZ] DE CARVALLO
Unplaced		Montevideo · Uruguayan · found in Montevideo
HUSBAND OF DOMINGA LLERENA, SANCHO'S DAUGHTER - established on an act 14 September 2026. A CAPTAIN, son of MAYOR DON JOAQUIN RUIZ DE CARVALLO and the late Dona Juana Suarez. Named in the Catedral baptism of their daughter Lucinda Gregoria, 12 May 1827. He is one half of the two-Dominga problem: a second indexed record gives a Dominga, daughter of Sancho and Maria Fernandez, married instead to an ANTONIO RODRIGUEZ. Both husbands are ANTONIO, which is suggestive and is not proof. THE TWO-DOMINGA PROBLEM IS CLOSED, AND HE IS THE REASON IT EXISTED. The baptism of his son DOMINGO on 25 October 1840 calls him "D. ANTONIO RODRIGUEZ DE CARBALLO" where the 1827 act called him "CAPITAN DON ANTONIO RUIZ DE CARVALLO" - and both give his father as JOAQUIN and his wife as a DOMINGA whose parents are SANCHO LERENA and MARIA FERNANDEZ. One man, one wife, two spellings, and an index that made him look like two husbands. The 1840 act adds that he was born in the PROVINCIA DEL RIO GRANDE, Brazil, and that his parents came from the COLONIA DEL SACRAMENTO. READ OFF THE PLATE 21 September 2026, where this row had rested on a MACHINE TRANSCRIPT. SEGUEIRA is a GALICIAN name - one more origin entering a family that already runs Andalusia, Asturias, Pernambuco and the Basque country. Nothing else here names him. Nothing matches that.

Why keep a list of people who are probably not us

Because the alternative is finding them twice. Every one of these cost a search, and a search that is not written down has to be paid for again. When a record turns up a Juan Carlos, or a Lerena at Rosario, or a family that moved between the two banks of the Plate, the question will be whether this archive has met them before — and this is the only page that can answer it. What has been read →

Last demonstrably alive — and how little that is known

This register records when a person was named. It has never recorded when they were last alive, and the two are not the same: a man named as a grandfather on an 1880 act may have been twenty years dead. Computed, never typed, by tools/last-alive.py.

The result is worth stating plainly. 23 of 356 people are known to have died at all. The remaining 333 carry a birth estimate and nothing more — a floor below which they certainly existed, not evidence they survived to anything later.

Two tiers, and the difference matters. DEATH is an explicit date on the person's own row. DEATH BY is a ceiling taken from a record that only exists because someone died — an estate or death-notice file — and it is never treated as the date: this archive has already been caught once, when Anton Armando died in April 2003 and his estate was advertised in 2013, ten years later.

The other archives

The Lerena Archive is one of seven, each following a different line of the same family. They use the same method and the same five words for how sure a thing is.

  • The Defranceschi Archive Istria · Kvarner · Carnia A family written down under eight sovereigns, in four languages, and spelled at least nine different ways. Defranceschi · De Franceschi · Defranceski · Defrančeski
  • The Falco Archive Arienzo, near Naples Nine documented generations born, married and buried in one valley, and then a migrant ship to Brisbane. Falco · Zampiello · Morgillo · Crisci · Liguori · Maione · Rivetti · Annecchino
  • The Blažević Archive Senj and the Krmpotska mountains An evidence-first archive for the family of Hedviga Blažević of Senj — carriers, a tobacco factory, and a wall grave. Blažević · Papić · Vukelić · Žubrinić · Kalanj
  • The Booyzen Archive Graaff-Reinet · Cradock · Johannesburg A Cape Dutch family that kept marrying British women, changed one letter of its name in a war, and came off the train at Johannesburg with nothing. Booyzen · Booysen · Kolbe · Barry · Downing · van Jaarsveld · Schoeman
  • The D'Arcy Archive England and Ireland · Parramatta · Brisbane Out with the 39th Regiment in 1825, and four generations afterwards in two Brisbane cemeteries. D'Arcy · Saniger · Sneyd · Hornby · Wakefield · Atkinson · Murdoch
  • The Mazza Archive Sicily and Calabria · Brisbane Four Italian quarters — Mazza of Piedimonte Etneo, and Prostamo, Arena and Polistena of Calabria — ending in four graves in a row at Nudgee. Mazza · Prostamo · Arena · Polistena

And for children: Our Family — The same ten families, told for a six-year-old.

An evidence-first family archive for the Lerena family — of Rosario and the River Plate, of the Cape and the Rand, and of Brisbane. Every claim carries its confidence. Living people are named — name and relationship only, never a date, a place, a photograph or a record.
